# Application

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Document Type | scope_srs |
| Scope | APP |
| Scope Name | Application |

## 1. Scope Overview

This scope covers application assembly, middleware, error handling, lifecycle and logging of NaruServe (issues #5, #6 and #7, wave 4): the explicit entry point `RestApp.builder()` and the `JsonCodec` SPI, the `RestApp.create()` runtime lookup of the bootstrap factory, the per-app `NEW`-state guard, `Middleware` and `Next` with global, prefix and route middleware, `onError` with framework HTTP errors and the standard error JSON body, `ServiceRegistry` and `ApplicationContext`, the lifecycle state machine with `listen()`, the start gate, `ServerHandle` and `shutdown`, the `Logger` SPI with the console logger, the access log, and the Logcat, rotating file and SLF4J loggers in their optional modules. It holds T35 (dispatcher part), T36, T37, T38 (HTTP part), T39, T40, T41, T42, T43, T45 and T46.

## 2. Scope Boundaries

### In Scope

- Public types of wave 4 in `NaruServe-api` under `com.snoworca.naruserve` and `com.snoworca.naruserve.spi`, and their internal implementation in `com.snoworca.naruserve.internal`.
- The Logcat logger in `NaruServe-logcat`, the rotating file logger in `NaruServe-filelog` and the SLF4J adapter in `NaruServe-slf4j`.
- The (미확정) markers owned by #5, #6 and #7 in `docs/api/app.md`, `docs/api/middleware.md`, `docs/api/lifecycle.md`, `docs/api/logging.md`, `docs/api/routing.md`, `docs/api/express-migration.md` and `docs/api/README.md`.

### Out of Scope

- The bootstrap factory that assembles TinyHttpd and Gson (D-228), the end-to-end half of D-110, the `create()` half of D-169, D-402, the TinyHttpd keep-alive socket case of D-166 with T44 and the non-daemon TinyHttpd thread of D-167 (wave 5, `docs/research/naruserve-v1/waves/carried-into-wave-5.md`).
- `res.json`, `req.body`, `app.jsonCodec`, `TypeRef` and the Gson implementation (wave 5); the D-136 serialisation case is asserted in wave 5.
- Static mounts, multipart and temporary-file cleanup (wave 6); `app.ws`, SSE and the WebSocket and SSE cases of D-142 (wave 7, `docs/research/naruserve-v1/waves/carried-into-wave-7.md`).
- TinyHttpd transport-level rejections (D-400) and any change inside the TinyHttpd checkout.

## 3. Assumptions and Constraints

- Core code is engine-free code in `NaruServe-api`: it references no TinyHttpd, Servlet, Gson, SLF4J, `android.*` or Kotlin stdlib type, compiles to Java 8 bytecode and passes the wave 1 animal-sniffer API 21 checks.
- Public types follow the wave 1 conventions: nullability annotations on public signatures, `@FunctionalInterface` callbacks, Express-style short method names, no Kotlin keywords as method names, and new methods only on `RestApp`, `Request`, `Response` and the `HttpAdapter` SPI (I-014).
- Where the design leaves a choice open, the wave decisions and the residual section of `docs/research/naruserve-v1/waves/wave-4/design.md` apply; otherwise the implementer decides under standing rule C-07, following RFC 9110 and, between equally valid options, Express behaviour.
- Source of the requirements: `docs/research/naruserve-v1/waves/wave-4/design.md`, `docs/research/naruserve-v1/waves/wave-4/excerpt.md` and `object.md`.

## 4. Requirements


### IR-APP-001 — Explicit entry point RestApp.builder() and the JsonCodec SPI

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, app-assembly |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/5 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall offer in `NaruServe-api` the explicit initialisation entry point `RestApp.builder()`, a static interface method returning the public final nested class `RestApp.Builder` with `httpAdapter(HttpAdapter)`, `jsonCodec(JsonCodec)`, `logger(Logger)` and `build()`, where `build()` returns a new `RestApp` in state `NEW` that the internal `DefaultRestApp` implements, and shall declare the SPI interface `com.snoworca.naruserve.spi.JsonCodec` with exactly `<T> T decode(Reader reader, Type type) throws IOException` and `void encode(Object value, Writer writer) throws IOException`, with no Gson type in any signature.

#### Rationale

Covers D-111, D-112, D-182, the D-137 entry-point decision (name and methods), the D-169 explicit-entry-point default and the carried line that the public entry point constructs `DefaultRestApp`. Wave 5 adds `TypeRef`, the JSON request and response methods, `app.jsonCodec` and the Gson implementation (D-112).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `RestApp.builder().httpAdapter(a).jsonCodec(c).build()` returns a `RestApp` whose `state()` is `NEW` and whose class is the internal `DefaultRestApp`; two `build()` calls on one builder return two distinct apps. [D-111; wave-4 carried: public entry point constructs DefaultRestApp] Decision: the entry point is `RestApp.builder()` returning `RestApp.Builder`, a public final nested class whose setters return the builder. Decision: two `build()` calls on one builder share the configured adapter, codec and logger. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-1 SDS-AC-1]
- [x] AC-2: `build()` without `httpAdapter(...)` and `build()` without `jsonCodec(...)` each throw `IllegalStateException` whose message names the missing setter, and `httpAdapter(null)`, `jsonCodec(null)` and `logger(null)` each throw `NullPointerException`. [D-111; D-137] Decision: the adapter is checked first, so a builder missing both names `httpAdapter(...)`, and a `null` setter argument throws at the setter, not at `build()`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-1 SDS-AC-2]
- [x] AC-3: An app built without `logger(...)` has `app.logger()` returning an instance of the public console logger `com.snoworca.naruserve.ConsoleLogger` of IR-APP-006, and an app built with `logger(l)` has `app.logger()` returning `l`. [D-169; wave decision: RestApp.create()] Decision: each `build()` without `logger(...)` creates its own new `ConsoleLogger`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-1 SDS-AC-3]
- [x] AC-4: Reflection on `com.snoworca.naruserve.spi.JsonCodec` finds exactly the two abstract methods `<T> T decode(java.io.Reader, java.lang.reflect.Type) throws java.io.IOException` and `void encode(java.lang.Object, java.io.Writer) throws java.io.IOException`, and no parameter, return or exception type of the interface lies in a `com.google.gson` package. [D-182; D-112]
- [x] AC-5: `JsonCodec` and `RestApp.Builder` carry nullability annotations on every public parameter and return value, and a Kotlin test in `NaruServe-api` compiles and runs `RestApp.builder().httpAdapter(adapter).jsonCodec(codec).build()` with a Kotlin object implementing `JsonCodec`. [D-111; D-182; C-03] Decision: package `com.snoworca.naruserve.spi` is `@NullMarked` and the `value` parameter of `JsonCodec#encode` is `@Nullable`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-1 SDS-AC-4]

### IR-APP-002 — Middleware and Next interfaces and the middleware registration methods

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, middleware |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/5 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall declare in `com.snoworca.naruserve` the `@FunctionalInterface` `Middleware` with `void handle(Request req, Response res, Next next) throws Exception` and the interface `Next` with `void run()` and `void error(Throwable error)`, and shall add to `RestApp` the methods `use(Middleware)`, `use(String prefix, Middleware)`, one route-middleware overload per route registration method (`get`, `post`, `put`, `patch`, `delete`, `head`, `options` with `(String path, Middleware mw, RouteHandler h)`, `route(Method, String, Middleware, RouteHandler)` and `route(MethodSet, String, Middleware, RouteHandler)`), each returning the app, and to `RouteBuilder` the method `use(Middleware)` returning the builder, with no API that continues a chain from another thread.

#### Rationale

Covers D-115, D-116, D-119, D-128 and the D-137 decisions on multi-middleware overloads, `RouteBuilder.use(middleware)` and the return type of the route-middleware overloads. Only new methods are added (I-014). T35.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Reflection finds `Middleware` annotated `@FunctionalInterface` with the single abstract method `void handle(Request, Response, Next) throws Exception`, and `Next` with exactly the abstract methods `void run()` and `void error(Throwable)`; a Java lambda `(req, res, next) -> next.run()` compiles as a `Middleware`. [D-115; D-116]
- [x] AC-2: `RestApp` declares `use(Middleware)`, `use(String, Middleware)`, the seven overloads `get|post|put|patch|delete|head|options(String, Middleware, RouteHandler)` and `route(Method, String, Middleware, RouteHandler)` and `route(MethodSet, String, Middleware, RouteHandler)`, each returning `RestApp`, and every wave 3 `RestApp` method keeps its signature. The wave 3 surface test of IR-HTTP-005 AC-5 (a statement about the wave 3 merge) is re-pointed to the wave 4 method set: the wave 3 methods plus those of IR-APP-002, IR-APP-003, IR-APP-005, IR-APP-006 and IR-APP-007, with `create()` and `builder()` as the only static methods. [D-119; I-014; D-137] Decision: the route-middleware overloads return `RestApp` like the plain registration methods.
- [x] AC-3: `RouteBuilder` declares `use(Middleware)` returning `RouteBuilder`; middleware added through it applies as route middleware to every method the same builder registers after the `use` call, in `use` call order, so `app.route("/a").use(m1).use(m2).get(h)` runs `m1`, `m2`, then `h` for `GET /a`. [D-137; D-120] Decision: `RouteBuilder.use(middleware)` is adopted and is the way to attach several route middleware; no varargs or multi-middleware overload is added to `RestApp`.
- [x] AC-4: `use(null)`, `use("/api", null)`, `use(null, mw)` and every route-middleware overload with a `null` middleware or handler throw `NullPointerException`, and `use(prefix, mw)` with a prefix that does not start with `/` throws `IllegalArgumentException`. [D-118; D-119] Decision: each refused call throws before anything is recorded. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-1 SDS-AC-7] User-approved revision: FR-DX-008 overrides scope regrouping: global and matching prefix middleware execute in their combined registration order, then route middleware.
- [x] AC-5: No public type of `NaruServe-api` declares a method that resumes a middleware chain from another thread or returns a future, and `Next` has no method other than `run()` and `error(Throwable)`. [D-128]
- [x] AC-6: A Kotlin test in `NaruServe-api` compiles and runs `app.use { req, res, next -> next.run() }`, `app.use("/api") { req, res, next -> next.run() }` and `app.get("/x", mw) { req, res -> res.send("x") }` without a DSL. [D-115; C-03]

### IR-APP-003 — onError, ErrorHandler and the framework HTTP error types

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, error-handling |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/5 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall declare the `@FunctionalInterface` `ErrorHandler` with `void handle(Throwable error, Request req, Response res) throws Exception`, add `RestApp onError(ErrorHandler handler)`, and expose the HTTP status of framework-raised errors through the public unchecked exception `com.snoworca.naruserve.HttpException` with `int status()` and `String code()`, plus its subclass `UnsupportedMediaTypeException` for `415`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-129 (interface part), D-130 and the D-137 decisions on the callback interface name and `throws Exception`, and the wave decision on `415` (the framework exception type for `415`; the body readers of waves 5 and 6 raise it). Apps may throw `HttpException` themselves, which gives the explicit `422` of D-134.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Reflection finds `ErrorHandler` annotated `@FunctionalInterface` with the single abstract method `void handle(Throwable, Request, Response) throws Exception`, and `RestApp.onError(ErrorHandler)` returning `RestApp`; `app.onError((error, req, res) -> res.status(500).send("x"))` compiles in Java and `app.onError { error, req, res -> }` in a Kotlin test. [D-129; D-137] Decision: the callback interface is `ErrorHandler` and it may throw `Exception`.
- [x] AC-2: `HttpException` extends `RuntimeException`, has public constructors `(int status)`, `(int status, String message)` and `(int status, String message, Throwable cause)`, and `status()` returns the given status; a status outside `400..599` throws `IllegalArgumentException`. [D-130; D-134] Decision: framework errors expose their status through `HttpException#status()`. Decision: the `IllegalArgumentException` message names the refused status. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-1 SDS-AC-9]
- [x] AC-3: `HttpException#code()` returns the `code` value of FR-APP-006 for its status (for example `BAD_REQUEST` for `400`, `UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY` for `422`). [D-130; D-137]
- [x] AC-4: `new UnsupportedMediaTypeException()` and `new UnsupportedMediaTypeException("m")` are `HttpException`s with `status()` `415` and `code()` `UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE`. [D-134; wave decision: 415]
- [x] AC-5: `onError(null)` throws `NullPointerException`, and `onError` is a registration method under the `NEW`-state guard of FR-APP-002. [D-114; D-129]

### IR-APP-004 — ServiceRegistry, ApplicationContext and the request service accessors

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, services |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/6 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall declare in `com.snoworca.naruserve` the interface `ServiceRegistry` with `<T> void register(Class<T> type, T instance)`, `<T> void registerExternal(Class<T> type, T instance)`, `<T> T get(Class<T> type)`, `<T> T getOrNull(Class<T> type)` and `boolean contains(Class<?> type)`, the unchecked `ServiceNotFoundException`, and the interface `ApplicationContext extends ServiceRegistry` with `void attribute(String key, Object value)`, `<T> T attribute(String key, Class<T> type)`, `void own(AutoCloseable resource)` and `Logger logger()`, and shall add to `Request` the methods `ApplicationContext application()` and `<T> T service(Class<T> type)`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-138, D-142 (interface part), D-145, D-146 and the D-151 decision (`registerExternal` registers an object without automatic close). Only new methods are added to `Request` (I-014).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Reflection finds on `ServiceRegistry` exactly `register(Class, Object)`, `registerExternal(Class, Object)`, `get(Class)`, `getOrNull(Class)` and `contains(Class)` with the generic signatures of the requirement, and on `ApplicationContext` (a subinterface of `ServiceRegistry`) `attribute(String, Object)`, `attribute(String, Class)`, `own(AutoCloseable)` and `logger()`. [D-138; D-151] Decision: an externally owned object is registered with `registerExternal(Class<T>, T)`, which the framework never closes.
- [x] AC-2: `get(type)` for a type that is not registered throws `ServiceNotFoundException` (a `RuntimeException`) whose message names the type, and `getOrNull(type)` returns `null` and `contains(type)` returns `false` for it. [D-138] Decision: the `ServiceNotFoundException` message contains the binary name of the type. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-2 SDS-AC-1]
- [x] AC-3: `Request` declares `ApplicationContext application()` and `<T> T service(Class<T> type)`, and every wave 3 `Request` method keeps its signature. The wave 3 surface test of IR-HTTP-001 AC-3 (a statement about the wave 3 merge) is re-pointed so that `service` and `application` are expected on `Request` from wave 4. [D-142; I-014]
- [x] AC-4: `register`, `registerExternal` and `own` with a `null` argument, `get`, `getOrNull` and `contains` with a `null` type, and `attribute` with a `null` key throw `NullPointerException`; the value parameter of `attribute(String, Object)` is `@Nullable` and storing a typed `null` variable (`Object v = null; ctx.attribute(key, v)`) removes the key, because a literal `null` resolves to the read overload; `attributeAs(key, type)` throws `ClassCastException` when the stored value is not an instance of `type`, as the request attribute store of FR-HTTP-012 does. [D-138; D-144]
- [x] AC-5: The new types carry nullability annotations on public signatures (`getOrNull` and `attribute(String, Class)` `@Nullable`, `get` and `service` `@NonNull`), and a Kotlin test compiles `ctx.register(UserService::class.java, s)` and `req.service(UserService::class.java)` with a non-null result type. [D-138; C-03] Decision: of these signatures only the returns of `getOrNull` and `attribute(String, Class)` are `@Nullable`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-2 SDS-AC-4]

### IR-APP-005 — Lifecycle API: AppState, onStart/onStop, listen, ServerHandle and shutdown

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, lifecycle |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/6 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall declare the enum `AppState` with `NEW`, `STARTING`, `RUNNING`, `STOPPING`, `FAILED` and `TERMINATED`, the `@FunctionalInterface` `LifecycleCallback` with `void handle(ApplicationContext ctx) throws Exception`, the interface `ServerHandle` with `int port()`, `String address()` and `AppState state()`, the unchecked `AppStartException`, and add to `RestApp` the methods `onStart(LifecycleCallback)` and `onStop(LifecycleCallback)` returning the app, `ServerHandle listen(int port)`, `void shutdown(long timeout, TimeUnit unit)` and `AppState state()`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-152, D-155 (accessor names decided for #6), the D-159 and D-426 exception type decided for #6 and the D-137 callback-name decision for `onStart`/`onStop`. `listen` binds to `127.0.0.1` (localhost only, `docs/api/app.md`); the LAN binding API stays with a later wave.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `AppState.values()` is exactly `NEW, STARTING, RUNNING, STOPPING, FAILED, TERMINATED` in that order, and `app.state()` of a newly built app is `NEW`. [D-152]
- [x] AC-2: Reflection finds `LifecycleCallback` annotated `@FunctionalInterface` with the single abstract method `void handle(ApplicationContext) throws Exception`, and `RestApp.onStart(LifecycleCallback)` and `RestApp.onStop(LifecycleCallback)` returning `RestApp`; `onStart(null)` and `onStop(null)` throw `NullPointerException`. [D-137] Decision: `onStart` and `onStop` share the callback interface `LifecycleCallback`, which may throw `Exception`.
- [x] AC-3: `ServerHandle` declares exactly `int port()`, `String address()` and `AppState state()`; after `listen(0)` succeeded `port()` returns the adapter's `boundPort()`, `address()` returns `127.0.0.1` and `state()` returns the app's current state, so it reads `TERMINATED` after shutdown. [D-155] Decision: the accessors are `port()`, `address()` and `state()`, and `listen` binds to `127.0.0.1`.
- [x] AC-4: `listen(port)` with a port outside `0..65535` throws `IllegalArgumentException` and leaves the state `NEW`; `shutdown(timeout, unit)` with a negative timeout throws `IllegalArgumentException` and with a `null` unit `NullPointerException`. [D-153; D-161] Decision: the argument checks of `listen` and `shutdown` run before any state change. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-2 SDS-AC-6]
- [x] AC-5: `AppStartException` extends `RuntimeException`; it is the type `listen()` throws for a checked start-failure cause (as its cause) and for a start ended by a pending stop. [D-159; D-426] Decision: the unchecked wrapper type is `AppStartException`. Decision: `AppStartException` has a public `(String message, Throwable cause)` constructor. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-2 SDS-AC-7]
- [x] AC-6: A Kotlin test compiles and runs `app.onStart { ctx -> ctx.attribute("k", 1) }`, `val h = app.listen(0)`, `h.port()`, `app.state()` and `app.shutdown(1, TimeUnit.SECONDS)`. [D-152; D-155; C-03]

### IR-APP-006 — Logger SPI, LogLevel, the console logger and app.logger

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, logging |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/7 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall declare in `com.snoworca.naruserve` the interface `Logger` with exactly `trace(String)`, `debug(String)`, `info(String)`, `warn(String)`, `error(String)` and `error(String, Throwable)`, the enum `LogLevel` (`TRACE`, `DEBUG`, `INFO`, `WARN`, `ERROR`), and the public console logger `ConsoleLogger implements Logger, Flushable, AutoCloseable` that is the default app logger of `RestApp.builder()`, and shall add to `RestApp` the methods `Logger logger()` and `RestApp logger(Logger logger)`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-168, D-169 (the console logger and its default for the explicit entry point; the `create()` half is wave 5), D-170 and the D-181 decision on the console line format. The wave decision on logger shutdown keeps the six `Logger` methods.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Reflection finds on `Logger` exactly the six abstract methods `trace(String)`, `debug(String)`, `info(String)`, `warn(String)`, `error(String)` and `error(String, Throwable)`, and `LogLevel.values()` is `TRACE, DEBUG, INFO, WARN, ERROR`. [D-168; wave decision: Logger shutdown]
- [x] AC-2: `new ConsoleLogger()` writes `info`, `warn` and `error` records and drops `trace` and `debug`; `new ConsoleLogger(LogLevel.TRACE)` writes all five levels. Each record is one line `<timestamp> <LEVEL> <message>` where the timestamp is formatted with `SimpleDateFormat("yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss.SSSZ")` (offset like `+0900`; no `java.time`, no `X` pattern, both absent from Android API 21), checked by a test under two time zones, `TRACE`/`DEBUG`/`INFO` go to `System.out` and `WARN`/`ERROR` to `System.err`, and `error(m, t)` writes the stack trace of `t` after the line. [D-169; D-181] Decision: the console logger defaults to threshold `INFO` and uses this line format. Decision: the console logger reads `System.out` and `System.err` at write time, so a stream replaced through `System.setOut` or `System.setErr` receives later records. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-2 SDS-AC-9]
- [x] AC-3: `ConsoleLogger#close()` and `flush()` flush both streams and leave `System.out` and `System.err` open, so a later `System.out.println` still prints. [wave decision: Logger shutdown]
- [x] AC-4: `app.logger(l)` makes `app.logger()` return `l`, `app.logger()` before any call returns the builder's logger (the console logger by default), and `app.logger(null)` throws `NullPointerException`; `logger(l)` is under the `NEW`-state guard of FR-APP-002 while `logger()` is readable in every state. [D-170; D-114]
- [x] AC-5: `ConsoleLogger` is in `NaruServe-api` and references no SLF4J or `android.*` type. [D-169; D-178]

### IR-APP-007 — Access log configuration API

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, access-log |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/7 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall declare the generic `@FunctionalInterface` `Configurer<T>` with `void configure(T target)` and the configuration type `AccessLogOptions` with `enabled(boolean)`, `level(LogLevel)`, `includeRequestId(boolean)`, `includeDuration(boolean)`, `includeBytes(boolean)` and `exclude(String path)`, each returning the options, and add `RestApp accessLog(Configurer<AccessLogOptions> configurer)` with the defaults enabled, level `INFO`, request id, duration and bytes included, and no excluded path.

#### Rationale

Covers D-172 and the D-181 decision on the option defaults. `Configurer<T>` avoids `java.util.function` in the public API (Android API 21) and is the shape later `multipart(config -> ...)` and `staticFiles(..., options -> ...)` reuse.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `app.accessLog(options -> options.enabled(true).level(LogLevel.DEBUG).includeRequestId(false).includeDuration(false).includeBytes(false).exclude("/health"))` compiles and returns the app, and each option method returns the same `AccessLogOptions` instance. [D-172]
- [x] AC-2: Without any `accessLog` call the access log is enabled at level `INFO` with request id, duration and bytes included and no excluded path; two `accessLog` calls apply both configurers to the same options in call order. [D-181; D-172] Decision: these are the defaults, and `accessLog` calls accumulate.
- [x] AC-3: `accessLog(null)`, `level(null)` and `exclude(null)` throw `NullPointerException`, and `accessLog` is under the `NEW`-state guard of FR-APP-002. [D-172; D-114]
- [x] AC-4: `Configurer` is annotated `@FunctionalInterface`, and a Kotlin test compiles `app.accessLog { it.exclude("/health") }`. [D-172; C-03]

### FR-APP-001 — RestApp.create() looks up the bootstrap AppFactory through ServiceLoader

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, app-assembly |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/5 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall provide the static interface method `RestApp.create()` that looks up the engine-free SPI `com.snoworca.naruserve.spi.AppFactory` (`RestApp create()`) with `ServiceLoader.load(AppFactory.class, RestApp.class.getClassLoader())` through a helper in `com.snoworca.naruserve.internal` and returns the app the single provider creates, throws an exception whose message names `com.snoworca.naruserve:NaruServe` when no provider is present and an exception naming every provider class when more than one is present.

#### Rationale

Covers D-110 (declaration and lookup; the bootstrap factory and the end-to-end half are wave 5, `carried-into-wave-5.md`), the D-226 lookup and D-227 missing-module exception delivered by wave 4 (wave decision RestApp.create(), W2-001) and the residual on the class-loader overload (the helper lives in `internal`; `RestApp` gains no overload).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `RestApp.create()` is a `public static` method of the `RestApp` interface returning `RestApp`, and `RestApp` declares no other `create` overload. [D-110; residual: class-loader overload]
- [x] AC-2: Through the internal lookup helper and a dedicated class loader that exposes exactly one fake provider at `META-INF/services/com.snoworca.naruserve.spi.AppFactory`, the helper returns the app the provider's `create()` returned. [D-226; wave decision: RestApp.create()] Decision: the lookup uses `ServiceLoader.load(AppFactory.class, loader)` where `RestApp.create()` passes `RestApp.class.getClassLoader()` and never the thread context class loader.
- [x] AC-3: With a class loader that exposes no provider, the helper throws `IllegalStateException` whose message contains `com.snoworca.naruserve:NaruServe` and mentions `RestApp.builder()`; `RestApp.create()` itself throws that exception in the `NaruServe-api` test run, where no provider is on the class path. [D-227]
- [x] AC-4: With a class loader that exposes two providers, the helper throws `IllegalStateException` whose message names both provider class names. [wave decision: RestApp.create()]
- [x] AC-5: The fake providers live in the `NaruServe-api` `test` source set, not in its `testFixtures`, so no other module's tests see a provider, and `NaruServe-api` main has no compile-time reference to TinyHttpd or Gson. The provider classes are compiled in `src/test/java`, but their `META-INF/services/com.snoworca.naruserve.spi.AppFactory` file is not on the test class path: the test writes it into a temporary directory that only the dedicated class loader sees. [D-226; wave decision: RestApp.create()] Decision: the dedicated class loader is a `URLClassLoader` over the temporary directory whose parent loader holds the nested fake provider classes. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-1 SDS-AC-15]

### FR-APP-002 — Per-app state isolation and the NEW-state guard

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, app-assembly |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/5 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall keep each `RestApp`'s configuration, routes, middleware, service registry, application context and logger in that instance with no mutable static state shared between instances, and shall reject every registration and configuration method of `RestApp` and `RouteBuilder` with `IllegalStateException` when the app state is not `NEW`, through one internal state guard that later waves call for `ws`, `staticFiles`, `multipart` and `jsonCodec`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-113, D-114 and the carried rule that the wave 3 after-freeze `IllegalStateException` becomes the `NEW`-state rule of D-114 and D-152.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Two apps built in one JVM with different routes, middleware, loggers and `onStart` registrations each answer only their own routes, run only their own middleware, log only to their own logger and expose only their own services and attributes. [D-113]
- [x] AC-2: A reflection scan finds no non-`final` static field and no static field of a mutable collection type in the classes of `com.snoworca.naruserve` and `com.snoworca.naruserve.internal` other than compile-time constants and `private static final` arrays that are never written after class initialisation (such as `MethodSet` `METHODS` and `DefaultResponse` `EMPTY`). [D-113]
- [x] AC-3: In each of the states `STARTING` (called from an `onStart` callback), `RUNNING`, `STOPPING` (called from an `onStop` callback) and `TERMINATED`, each of `get`, `post`, `put`, `patch`, `delete`, `head`, `options`, `route(Method, ...)`, `route(MethodSet, ...)`, their route-middleware overloads, `use(mw)`, `use(prefix, mw)`, `accessLog`, `logger(l)`, `onStart`, `onStop`, `onError`, `routerPolicy` and the `get`/`use` methods of a `RouteBuilder` obtained in `NEW` throw `IllegalStateException` whose message names the method and the current state, and the refused call changes nothing. [D-114; wave-4 carried: NEW-state rule]
- [x] AC-4: Every guarded method calls one internal guard method, so a test that lists by reflection all non-static `RestApp` methods returning `RestApp` (including `logger(Logger)`) and the registration methods of a `RouteBuilder` obtained from `route(String)` finds each one rejected in `RUNNING`, while `logger()`, `state()` and `route(String)` itself are not guarded. [D-114] Decision: the one guard is the internal `AppLifecycle.requireConfigurable`, called first by every guarded method. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-3 SDS-AC-4]
- [x] AC-5: The after-freeze rejection of verified FR-HTTP-008 AC-5 still holds, because the one guard rejects when the state is not `NEW` or the route table is frozen (the harness and the FR-HTTP-008 tests freeze while the state is still `NEW`); FR-HTTP-008 AC-6 states the wave 3 situation that D-111 ends, and its wave 3 test is re-pointed to assert that `RestApp.builder()` and `RestApp.create()` are the only public static methods of `com.snoworca.naruserve` that return a `RestApp` or its builder. [D-114; D-111; wave-4 carried: NEW-state rule]

### FR-APP-003 — Middleware scope and pipeline order in the dispatcher

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, middleware |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/5 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall execute global and matching segment-boundary prefix middleware in their combined registration order, followed by matched route middleware and its handler, or the automatic/static fallback. All continuations remain synchronous and single-use.

#### Rationale

Covers D-117, D-118, D-120, D-121 and the wave decision on pre-request rejections (middleware runs for `404`, `405`, `426`, the automatic `OPTIONS` reply and the `501` of a disabled `TRACE` or `CONNECT`, including `CONNECT /`; the static-file and WebSocket-upgrade cases are asserted in waves 6 and 7). T35.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: With global middleware `g1`, `g2`, prefix middleware `p1` on `/api` registered before `g2` and `p2` on `/api`, and `app.route("/api/users").use(r1).use(r2).get(h)`, a `GET /api/users` records `g1, g2, p1, p2, r1, r2, h`, and `app.get("/b", r3, h2)` makes `GET /b` record `g1, g2, r3, h2`. [D-120; D-119; T35] Decision: all global middleware run first in registration order, then all matching prefix middleware in registration order, however the two kinds of registration interleave. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-4 SDS-AC-1] User-approved revision: FR-DX-008 overrides scope regrouping: global and matching prefix middleware execute in their combined registration order, then route middleware.
- [x] AC-2: Prefix `/api` runs for `/api` and `/api/users` and not for `/apix` or `/ap`; prefix `/api/` behaves as `/api`, and prefix `/` runs for every path. [D-118; T35] Decision: a trailing `/` of a prefix is ignored and `/` covers every path. User-approved revision: FR-DX-008 overrides scope regrouping: global and matching prefix middleware execute in their combined registration order, then route middleware.
- [x] AC-3: With `RouterPolicy.builder().caseInsensitive(true)` the `/api` prefix middleware runs for `/API/users`, and with `mergeSlashes(true)` it runs for `//api//users`, so every path that reaches a route under the prefix runs the prefix middleware. [D-118] Decision: the prefix comparison uses the same case and slash normalisation that the router applies under the app's `RouterPolicy`. User-approved revision: FR-DX-008 overrides scope regrouping: global and matching prefix middleware execute in their combined registration order, then route middleware.
- [x] AC-4: Inside prefix middleware and the handler, `req.path()` still returns the full decoded path (for example `/api/users`, not `/users`). [D-118; D-137] Decision: NaruServe does not strip the mount prefix from `req.path()` and has no `baseUrl`. User-approved revision: FR-DX-008 overrides scope regrouping: global and matching prefix middleware execute in their combined registration order, then route middleware.
- [x] AC-5: A request that matches no route runs global and matching prefix middleware and then receives the `404` of the fallback point; route middleware of other routes does not run. [D-121; D-117] Decision: for a lookup result other than `MATCHED` the dispatcher builds the `Request` with the parsed `Method` and empty params and runs the global and matching prefix middleware with an empty route middleware list and the fallback answer as the terminal step. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-4 SDS-AC-6] User-approved revision: FR-DX-008 overrides scope regrouping: global and matching prefix middleware execute in their combined registration order, then route middleware.
- [x] AC-6: A recording global middleware runs once each for a `405` (method mismatch), a `426` (WebSocket-only route without upgrade), an automatic `OPTIONS /r` `204`, a `501` for `TRACE /x` with `TRACE` disabled and a `501` for `CONNECT /`, before the automatic response is written. [D-117; wave decision: Pre-request rejections] User-approved revision: FR-DX-008 overrides scope regrouping: global and matching prefix middleware execute in their combined registration order, then route middleware.
- [x] AC-7: A global middleware does not run for the pre-request rejections (unrecognised method token `501`, raw path not starting with `/` `400`, `PathValidator` rejection `400`, `OPTIONS *` `204`) nor for the lifecycle-gate `503`s of FR-APP-009 and FR-APP-013. [wave decision: Pre-request rejections; wave decision: Lifecycle gate] User-approved revision: FR-DX-008 overrides scope regrouping: global and matching prefix middleware execute in their combined registration order, then route middleware.
- [x] AC-8: For a matched route all middleware and the handler run on the adapter thread that called the dispatcher, one after the other. [D-128]

### FR-APP-004 — next.run() contract

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, middleware |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/5 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall let each middleware invocation continue the chain at most once through `next.run()` or `next.error(e)`, return from `next.run()` only after every downstream middleware and the handler returned, deliver the response of a middleware that responds without calling `next.run()` with no later middleware or handler run, and throw `IllegalStateException` for a second continuation within one invocation and for a continuation after the response completed or the request ended.

#### Rationale

Covers D-122, D-123, D-124, D-125 and D-128 (synchronous execution). The unfinished-response case of D-126 is in the wave 4 successor of FR-HTTP-006. T35, T36.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A middleware that calls `next.run()` twice gets `IllegalStateException` from the second call, and the downstream middleware and handler ran once. [D-122; T35]
- [x] AC-2: A middleware that calls `next.run()` after `res.send("x")` completed the response gets `IllegalStateException` and no downstream middleware runs; a `Next` kept and called after the request ended (from the test thread after the in-memory exchange finished) throws `IllegalStateException`. [D-123] Decision: `next.run()` throws when the response is `COMPLETED` or `ABORTED` or the request has ended, and no downstream step runs. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-4 SDS-AC-10]
- [x] AC-3: A middleware that records a marker after `next.run()` returns sees the handler's marker already recorded and `res.isCommitted()` true for a handler that called `res.send(...)`. [D-124] Decision: an exception thrown downstream propagates out of `next.run()` unchanged and the dispatcher hands it to the error path once, so a middleware may wrap `next.run()` in `try`/`catch`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-4 SDS-AC-11]
- [x] AC-4: A middleware that writes `res.status(401).send("no")` and returns without `next.run()` delivers `401` with body `no`, and the later middleware and the handler record no call. [D-125; T36]
- [x] AC-5: Calling `next.error(e)` after `next.run()` in the same invocation, or `next.run()` after `next.error(e)`, throws `IllegalStateException`; `next.error(null)` throws `NullPointerException`. [D-122; D-116] Decision: `next.run()` and `next.error(e)` together count as the single continuation of one invocation. Decision: `next.error(null)` throws before the continuation is consumed. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-4 SDS-AC-12]
- [x] AC-6: `next.run()` runs the downstream chain on the calling thread, and no middleware or handler runs after the dispatcher returned to the adapter for a non-streaming response. [D-128]

### FR-APP-005 — Unified error path and onError chain

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, error-handling |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/5 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall route an exception thrown by a middleware or handler and an error passed to `next.error(e)` to one error path that calls the registered `onError` handlers in registration order while the previous one left the response `NEW`, writes the standard error response of FR-APP-006 when the response is still `NEW` after them, and aborts instead of writing an error body when the response is already committed but not completed, while `onError` never receives the automatic `404`, `405`, `426`, `501` or `OPTIONS` responses.

#### Rationale

Covers D-127, D-129, D-131, the D-137 decision on `onError` chaining, D-136 (asserted in wave 5 once `res.json` exists) and the residual on `onError` scope. Replaces the wave 3 interim failure handling (FR-HTTP-006, superseded in wave 4). T36, T37.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: With one recording `onError`, a `RuntimeException` thrown by a middleware, a checked `IOException` thrown by a handler and `next.error(e)` from a middleware each reach the handler once with the same `Throwable` instance and the request's `Request` and `Response`. [D-127; D-129; T36] Decision: a thrown `java.lang.Error` is not routed to `onError`; it aborts the response and is rethrown, as in wave 3. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-5 SDS-AC-1]
- [x] AC-2: An `HttpException(400)` thrown by a handler, an `HttpException(413)` and an `UnsupportedMediaTypeException` each reach `onError` with `status()` readable, and the malformed query `?x=%zz` raises an `HttpException` with status `400` that reaches `onError` after the middleware ran and before the handler. [D-129; D-130; wave-4 carried: malformed query]
- [x] AC-3: With two `onError` handlers, the second runs only when the first returned with the response `NEW`, and receives the same error; when the first responded, the second records no call. [D-137] Decision: `onError` registrations form one chain in registration order that stops at the first handler that starts the response. Decision: the first `onError` handler runs whatever the response state was when the error arrived; a later one runs only while the response is still `NEW`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-5 SDS-AC-2]
- [x] AC-4: When every `onError` handler returned with the response `NEW`, or none is registered, the response is the standard error response of FR-APP-006 for the error. [D-132; D-137] Decision: the standard error response drops the status and headers the app set before the error. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-5 SDS-AC-3]
- [x] AC-5: An `onError` handler that throws makes the framework log the thrown exception at `ERROR` through the app logger and answer the standard `500` error response if the response is still `NEW`; no later `onError` handler runs. [D-129; D-133]
- [x] AC-6: When a handler throws while the response is `STREAMING`, `onError` is still called once, no error body is written, and after the error path the response is `ABORTED` with the in-memory exchange closed abnormally; when the response is `COMPLETED`, it stays `COMPLETED` with the sent bytes unchanged. [D-131; T37]
- [x] AC-7: A recording `onError` records no call for the automatic `404`, `405`, `426`, `501` and `OPTIONS` responses and for the pre-request and lifecycle-gate rejections. [residual: onError scope; wave decision: Pre-request rejections]
- [x] AC-8: An error that ends in a standard `500` response is logged once at `ERROR` with its `Throwable` through the app logger; an `HttpException` with a `4xx` status is not logged at `ERROR`. [D-129; D-133] Decision: only `500`s are logged at `ERROR`, with the stack trace kept in the server log. Decision: a `500` is logged at `ERROR` whatever the response state, and no other status is logged at `ERROR`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-5 SDS-AC-6]
- [x] AC-9: The error path decides by the response state at the time the error arrives (`NEW` gives the error response, committed but not completed gives an abort, `COMPLETED` stays unchanged), the rule that the `res.json` serialisation case of D-136 relies on; that case itself is asserted in wave 5 once `res.json` exists. [D-136; D-131] Decision: the error path reads the response state once, after the `onError` chain, under the response lock; because a response committed on arrival can only stay committed or end, this gives the same outcome as the arrival-state rule. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-5 SDS-AC-7]

### FR-APP-006 — Standard error JSON body, status mapping and code values

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, error-handling |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/5 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall answer an error with no `onError` response, and the framework's own error statuses `400`, `404`, `405`, `426`, `501` and `503`, with the body `{"error":{"code":...,"message":...,"requestId":...}}`, `Content-Type: application/json; charset=UTF-8` and a `Content-Length`, using the status mapping of D-134 and the `code` values decided for #5, with no stack trace or exception class name and with `requestId` equal to the id the access log records for the request.

#### Rationale

Covers D-132, D-133, D-134, D-135, the D-137 decisions on `code` values per status and on custom `404`/`405` bodies, the wave decisions on `415`, on `426` and `501` (automatic responses with the standard body) and on overload `503` (a full TinyHttpd queue is a transport-level `503`, D-400, not standard JSON), and the carried line that wave 4 gives the error statuses the standard error body.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A handler that throws `new RuntimeException("secret com.example.Foo")` is answered `500` with exactly `{"error":{"code":"INTERNAL_ERROR","message":"Internal Server Error","requestId":"<id>"}}`, `Content-Type: application/json; charset=UTF-8` and a matching `Content-Length`, and the body contains neither the exception message, nor `RuntimeException`, nor `at ` stack frames. [D-132; D-133] Decision: the body has no whitespace and exactly the fields `code`, `message` and `requestId` in that order. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-5 SDS-AC-8]
- [x] AC-2: The `code` values are `400` `BAD_REQUEST`, `404` `NOT_FOUND`, `405` `METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED`, `413` `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`, `415` `UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE`, `422` `UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY`, `426` `UPGRADE_REQUIRED`, `500` `INTERNAL_ERROR`, `501` `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` and `503` `SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE`, and any other `HttpException` status uses `HTTP_<status>`. [D-134; D-137] Decision: these are the `code` values per status.
- [x] AC-3: The `message` is the status reason phrase (for example `Not Found`), except for an `HttpException` created with a message, whose message is used; a `500` always uses `Internal Server Error`. [D-133; D-137] Decision: only an app-supplied `HttpException` message reaches the client.
- [x] AC-4: Through the harness, a handler throwing `HttpException(400)`, `HttpException(422)`, `HttpException(413)` and `UnsupportedMediaTypeException` is answered `400`, `422`, `413` and `415` with the standard body, and any other exception `500`. [D-134; wave decision: 415]
- [x] AC-5: The automatic `404`, `405` (with its `Allow` header), `426` (with `Upgrade: websocket` and `Connection: Upgrade`), `501`, the pre-request `400`s and `501` and the lifecycle-gate `503`s carry the standard body with their `code`; the `204` answers keep no body. [D-134; wave decision: 426 and 501; wave-4 carried: standard error body]
- [x] AC-6: For a `HEAD` request the standard error response carries the headers and the `Content-Length` of the body it would have had and no body. [D-132; FR-HTTP-017]
- [x] AC-7: The `requestId` of a standard error body equals the request id field of the access log line of FR-APP-015 for the same request, also for the pre-request rejections. [D-135]
- [x] AC-8: The body is generated by the core without the app's `JsonCodec`, escapes `"`, `\` and control characters of the `message` per RFC 8259, and is identical with any codec. [D-132] Decision: the core writes the standard error JSON itself. Decision: non-ASCII characters stay literal UTF-8 and are not escaped. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-5 SDS-AC-13]
- [x] AC-9: No API replaces the automatic `404`/`405` bodies in v1; an app answers custom `404` pages with a route of its own. [D-137] Decision: custom `404`/`405` bodies are not configurable in v1.

### FR-APP-007 — Service registration, frozen registry and the single application context

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, services |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/6 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall accept `ctx.register` and `ctx.registerExternal` only while the app is `STARTING` and the `onStart` callbacks run, reject a second registration of the same `Class` key without replacing the first instance, freeze the registry into an immutable snapshot before `RUNNING`, run each `onStart` callback exactly once per successful `listen()`, keep exactly one `ApplicationContext` per app, which `onStart`, `onStop` and `req.application()` all receive, and return from `req.service(T)` the instance `req.application().get(T)` returns.

#### Rationale

Covers D-139, D-140, D-141, D-142 (HTTP handlers; the WebSocket and SSE cases are wave 7, `carried-into-wave-7.md`), D-143, D-144, D-145 and D-146. T41, T42.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A `UserService` registered in `onStart` is returned with identity by `req.service(UserService.class)` and `req.application().get(UserService.class)` in an HTTP handler; the WebSocket and SSE cases of D-142 are asserted in wave 7. [D-142; T41; wave decision: Services for long connections]
- [x] AC-2: `ctx.register` and `ctx.registerExternal` called from `onStop`, and `req.application().register(...)` called from a handler in `RUNNING`, throw `IllegalStateException`, and the registry is unchanged. [D-139; D-141; T42] Decision: `register`, `registerExternal` and `own` throw `IllegalStateException` naming the method and the state whenever the state is not `STARTING` or the registry is frozen, before the registry or the close list is touched. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-6 SDS-AC-2]
- [x] AC-3: A second `ctx.register(UserService.class, other)` in `onStart` throws `IllegalStateException` naming the key, so `listen()` fails with it, and `getOrNull(UserService.class)` read from a failing-start close hook still returns the first instance. [D-140; T42] Decision: the configuration error is `IllegalStateException`. Decision: the message contains the class name of the key, the first instance stays registered and the second is not tracked for closing. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-6 SDS-AC-3]
- [x] AC-4: In `RUNNING` sixteen threads calling `req.service(UserService.class)` concurrently all receive the registered instance, and the registry seen in `RUNNING` is an unmodifiable snapshot taken before the state became `RUNNING`. [D-141]
- [x] AC-5: Two `onStart` callbacks run in registration order, each exactly once for one successful `listen()`, and neither runs again on `shutdown`. [D-143; D-154; T41]
- [x] AC-6: `ctx.attribute` set in `onStart` is read by handlers in `RUNNING` and by `onStop` in `STOPPING`, and eight threads writing and reading distinct keys concurrently during `RUNNING` lose no write, and `onStop` writes `ctx.attribute("k2", v)` and reads it back. [D-144] Decision: attributes live in a concurrent map that the lifecycle gate does not guard. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-6 SDS-AC-7]
- [x] AC-7: `ctx.logger()` in `onStart` and `onStop` returns the same instance as `app.logger()`, also after `app.logger(l)` replaced it before `listen`. [D-145] Decision: `ctx.logger()` returns `app.logger()` read at call time, the logger as set and not the framework's failure-isolating wrapper. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-6 SDS-AC-8]
- [x] AC-8: The `ctx` passed to `onStart`, the `ctx` passed to `onStop` and `req.application()` in every request are the same instance. [D-146]

### FR-APP-008 — Owned and registered resources close once in reverse order

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, services |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/6 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall close every object passed to `ctx.own` and every `AutoCloseable` passed to `ctx.register` on shutdown and on start failure, exactly once per object compared by identity, in reverse order of each object's first `own` or `register` call, continuing after a failing `close()`, and shall never close an object registered only through `registerExternal`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-147, D-148, D-149, D-150 and the D-151 decision (`registerExternal`). T43, T45.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `ctx.own(db)`, then `ctx.register(UserService.class, users)` (an `AutoCloseable`) close as `users`, then `db` on shutdown. [D-147; D-149; T45]
- [x] AC-2: One object registered under an interface key and under its implementation key and also owned is closed exactly once, at the position of its first `own` or `register` call. [D-148; T45]
- [x] AC-3: When the second of three resources throws from `close()`, the first and third are still closed; on shutdown the failure is logged at `ERROR` through the app logger, and on start failure it is added as a suppressed exception to the exception `listen()` throws. [D-150; D-158] Decision: close failures are logged on shutdown and attached as suppressed on start failure.
- [x] AC-4: An object registered only with `registerExternal` is not closed on shutdown or start failure, while `get` returns it like any service; an object passed to both `registerExternal` and `own` is closed once. [D-151]
- [x] AC-5: A non-`AutoCloseable` service is registered and returned normally and nothing is closed for it. [D-149]

### FR-APP-009 — listen() startup sequence and the start gate

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, lifecycle |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/6 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall move the app from `NEW` to `STARTING` atomically in `listen()`, rejecting a second or concurrent `listen()`, then run the `onStart` callbacks in registration order, freeze and validate the router, registry and options with the wave 3 route-table freeze, bind and start accepting through the adapter in one call, enter `RUNNING` and return a `ServerHandle`, while the dispatcher holds every request that reaches it before `RUNNING` on a start gate, releases it into the pipeline once `RUNNING` is set and answers it `503` with `Connection: close` when the start fails or a pending stop ends it.

#### Rationale

Covers D-153, D-154, D-155, D-156, D-428, the wave decisions on `STARTING` and on the lifecycle gate (gate answers skip middleware and `onError` but get an access-log line and the standard error body) and the carried line that `listen()` runs the same route-table freeze as the wave 3 harness.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Two threads calling `listen(0)` at the same time on a `NEW` app: exactly one proceeds and the other gets `IllegalStateException`; a second `listen` after a successful one throws `IllegalStateException`. [D-153] Decision: the start is claimed by one compare-and-set from `NEW` to `STARTING`, and only its winner proceeds. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-3 SDS-AC-6]
- [x] AC-2: A recording test adapter and callbacks observe the order: state `STARTING`, `onStart` callbacks in registration order, route-table freeze, one `bind` call, state `RUNNING`, `listen` returns. [D-154; wave-4 carried: listen runs the route-table freeze]
- [x] AC-3: A duplicate route of D-044 makes `listen()` throw the freeze's `RoutingConfigException` after the `onStart` callbacks ran and before any `bind` call. [D-154; D-157]
- [x] AC-4: `listen()` returns only after `bind` returned, and the returned `ServerHandle` reports the bound port. [D-155]
- [x] AC-5: With a test adapter whose `bind` dispatches a request from another thread before returning, the request's middleware and handler run only after `app.state()` is `RUNNING` (asserted inside the handler), and the request is answered normally. [D-156; D-428] Decision: a request arriving in `STARTING` blocks on the start gate and is dispatched only after the state is set to `RUNNING`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-3 SDS-AC-10]
- [x] AC-6: With a test adapter whose `bind` dispatches a request from another thread and then throws `IOException`, the held request is answered `503` with `Connection: close` and the standard error body, and no middleware, handler or `onError` runs for it. [D-428; wave decision: Lifecycle gate; wave decision: STARTING]
- [x] AC-7: The test-fixtures harness `TestApp` builds its app with `RestApp.builder().httpAdapter(inMemoryAdapter).jsonCodec(stubCodec)` (a stub `JsonCodec` in the test fixtures) and its `start()` calls `listen(0)`, which runs the route-table freeze once, so a `RoutingConfigException` of the freeze still reaches the test caller unwrapped and every wave 3 test through `TestApp` that cites a requirement wave 4 does not supersede keeps passing unchanged, while the tests citing FR-HTTP-002, FR-HTTP-003, FR-HTTP-005, FR-HTTP-006 or FR-HTTP-010 are re-pointed to FR-HTTP-021..FR-HTTP-025 and assert the standard error body (a requirement change, not a weakened test); verified FR-HTTP-001 AC-3 (the harness builds the internal implementation and runs the freeze once) still holds. [wave-4 carried: listen runs the route-table freeze; D-154] Decision: the stub codec is the test-fixtures `StubJsonCodec`, and `start()` calls `listen(0)` once. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-3 SDS-AC-12]
- [x] AC-8: The start gate and the lifecycle gate belong to the exchange handler that `listen()` passes to the adapter; the wave 3 `Dispatcher(Router)` constructor and `DefaultRestApp.dispatcher()` that unit tests bind without `listen()` stay ungated, so those verified wave 3 unit tests keep dispatching. [D-428; D-156] Decision: the gates wrap the dispatcher instead of living inside it.

### FR-APP-010 — Start failure leaves no socket and closes resources

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, lifecycle |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/6 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall, when an `onStart` callback, the freeze validation or the adapter bind fails, leave no listening socket open, never run `onStop`, move the state to `FAILED`, close the already owned and registered resources in reverse order, flush and close the app logger in the final log-writer step, move to `TERMINATED` and throw the cause from `listen()`, an unchecked cause as is and a checked cause wrapped in `AppStartException`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-157, D-158, D-159, D-160 and the residual on closing the logger on every path into `TERMINATED` (failed start). T43.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: An `onStart` that throws `IllegalArgumentException` makes `listen()` throw that same instance, and the recording test adapter saw no `bind` call. [D-157; D-159; T43]
- [x] AC-2: An `onStart` that throws `IOException` makes `listen()` throw `AppStartException` whose cause is that `IOException`. [D-159]
- [x] AC-3: A bind failure (`bind` throws `IOException`) makes `listen()` throw `AppStartException` with that cause, after which the core called the adapter's `stop` and `release`, which the in-memory adapter and the recording test adapter accept without a bound socket, so the adapter holds no socket. [D-157; D-159] Decision: `stop` is called before `release`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-7 SDS-AC-3]
- [x] AC-4: Resources owned and registered before an `onStart` failure are closed in reverse order before `listen()` throws, and a resource's `close()` reads `app.state()` as `FAILED`; after `listen()` threw, `app.state()` is `TERMINATED`. [D-158; D-159; T43]
- [x] AC-5: No `onStop` callback runs for any start failure. [D-160]
- [x] AC-6: With a rotating file logger of FR-APP-017 as the app logger, no writer thread of it remains after a failed `listen()` (a test in `NaruServe-filelog`), and a console app logger that is also owned is closed once. [residual: Logger close on every path into TERMINATED; D-148; I-015] Decision: the app logger is flushed and closed once, as the last step before `TERMINATED`, and is skipped at its owned position. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-7 SDS-AC-6]

### FR-APP-011 — Shutdown sequence, deadline and shutdown participants

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, lifecycle |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/6 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall move a `RUNNING` app to `STOPPING` atomically on the first `shutdown(timeout, unit)` call, compute one deadline from the timeout and run in order the before-wait participants, the adapter stop up to the deadline, the force-close participants, the `onStop` callbacks once while services are still registered, the after-`onStop` participants, closing owned and registered resources in reverse order, flushing and closing the app logger, the adapter release and the move to `TERMINATED`, through an internal shutdown participant registry with these three fixed phases.

#### Rationale

Covers D-161, D-162, D-163, D-401, the wave decision on logger shutdown and the carried line that the adapter may force-close only at the stop deadline. Wave 6 adds temporary-file cleanup (D-255) as an after-`onStop` participant and wave 7 adds WebSocket (D-435) and SSE (D-436) closing as before-wait participants. T45, T46.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A recording test adapter, participants in all three phases, an `onStop` callback, an owned resource and a `Flushable`/`AutoCloseable` app logger record the order: before-wait participants, `adapter.stop`, force-close participants, `onStop`, after-`onStop` participants, owned resource `close`, logger `flush` and `close`, `adapter.release`, then `app.state()` is `TERMINATED`. [D-162; D-401; T46]
- [x] AC-2: The timeout passed to `adapter.stop` is the time left until the one deadline computed when `shutdown` began, so a before-wait participant that sleeps 200 ms of a 1 s timeout leaves at most 800 ms for the adapter stop. [D-162; wave-4 carried: exchange lifetime] Decision: the deadline is `System.nanoTime() + unit.toNanos(timeout)`, computed once, and `adapter.stop` receives `max(0, deadline - now)` nanoseconds. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-7 SDS-AC-8]
- [x] AC-3: The internal participant registry adds participants to a phase in order and runs them in that order; a participant that throws is logged at `ERROR` and the sequence continues. [D-401]
- [x] AC-4: `onStop` reads every service registered in `onStart` through `ctx.get`, before any owned resource is closed. [D-163; T45]
- [x] AC-5: Two threads calling `shutdown` on a `RUNNING` app: one runs the sequence; the other and any later call return without running any step again, and `onStop` ran once. [D-161; T46] Decision: the losing caller waits for `TERMINATED` up to its own timeout and then returns. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-7 SDS-AC-11]
- [x] AC-6: A request in flight when `shutdown` begins completes normally while the adapter stop waits within the deadline. [D-162]
- [x] AC-7: An app logger that is also owned is skipped at its owned position and closed once at the log-writer step; closing the console logger leaves `System.out` open. [wave decision: Logger shutdown; D-148]
- [x] AC-8: An `onStop` callback that throws is logged at `ERROR`, the remaining `onStop` callbacks run and the sequence continues to `TERMINATED`. [D-162] Decision: `onStop` failures do not stop shutdown. An `Error` (not only an `Exception`) thrown by an `onStop` callback or a shutdown participant is handled the same way, so the owned-resource close, the logger flush and close and the adapter release still run; after `TERMINATED` the first such `Error` is rethrown from `shutdown`, as on the start-failure path. [wave-4 loop P issue w4-S2-001]
- [x] AC-9: `shutdown(timeout, unit)` called from a route handler or middleware of a `RUNNING` app runs the sequence on a separate non-daemon thread named `naruserve-shutdown` and returns at once, and that request's response is sent in full; called from a handler while another shutdown is already `STOPPING`, it returns at once; when the shutdown thread cannot be started, the sequence runs on the calling thread and still reaches `TERMINATED`. [D-161; D-162; wave-4 loop P issue w4-P1-001] Decision: in-request shutdown does not wait for itself, because the sequence waits for in-flight requests.

### FR-APP-012 — shutdown in NEW, STARTING, FAILED and TERMINATED, and listen after termination

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, lifecycle |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/6 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall move a `NEW` app to `TERMINATED` on `shutdown` without running `onStart` or `onStop`, record a pending stop for a `shutdown` during `STARTING` and wait up to its timeout for `listen()` to end, which then finishes the step in progress, closes any bound socket, never enters `RUNNING`, never runs `onStop`, closes resources in reverse order and then flushes and closes the app logger, passes through `FAILED` to `TERMINATED` and throws `AppStartException`, return normally when that timeout passes first, wait up to the timeout for a `FAILED` app to reach `TERMINATED`, return at once for a `TERMINATED` app, and reject `listen()` on a `TERMINATED` app.

#### Rationale

Covers D-164, D-165, D-426, D-427, D-438 and the residual on closing the logger on every path into `TERMINATED` (stop while starting, shutdown before start). T46.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `shutdown` on a `NEW` app sets `TERMINATED`, runs no `onStart` or `onStop` callback, never calls the adapter's `bind`, and closes a `Flushable`/`AutoCloseable` app logger once. [D-164; residual: Logger close on every path into TERMINATED]
- [x] AC-2: With `onStart` blocked on a latch inside `listen()` on thread A, `shutdown(5, SECONDS)` on thread B, then the latch released: `onStart` finishes, no `bind` happens, `listen()` throws `AppStartException`, no `onStop` runs, owned resources close in reverse order and observe `FAILED`, `app.state()` ends `TERMINATED`, and `shutdown` returns after `listen()` ended, and a `Flushable`/`AutoCloseable` app logger is flushed and closed once after the resources closed and before `TERMINATED`. [D-426; T46; residual: Logger close on every path into TERMINATED] Decision: a start ended by a pending stop throws `AppStartException` with a message saying shutdown was requested while starting. Decision: with a pending stop recorded during `onStart` callback k, the callbacks registered after k do not run; the latch test registers a second callback and asserts it never ran.
- [x] AC-3: When the pending stop is recorded during `bind`, the socket bound by it is released (`stop` and `release` called) and `RUNNING` is never set, and a request that the test adapter dispatched during that `bind` is answered `503` with `Connection: close` and the standard error body with no middleware, handler or `onError` run. [D-426; D-428]
- [x] AC-4: With `onStart` blocked on a latch, `shutdown(100, MILLISECONDS)` returns normally without an exception before the latch is released, `app.state()` is then `STARTING` or `FAILED`, and once the latch is released `listen()` still passes through `FAILED` to `TERMINATED` and throws `AppStartException`. [D-438; T46]
- [x] AC-5: A resource `close()` running during a failing `listen()` (state `FAILED`) starts thread B, which calls `shutdown(5, SECONDS)`, and then blocks 100 ms; B's `shutdown` returns after the state became `TERMINATED` and within its timeout; `shutdown` on a `TERMINATED` app returns at once with no callback or close run. [D-427]
- [x] AC-6: `listen(0)` on a `TERMINATED` app throws `IllegalStateException`. [D-165; T46]

| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/6 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall answer every request that reaches the dispatcher after `STOPPING` began with `503`, `Connection: close` and the standard error body, at the lifecycle gate before the pipeline, so no middleware, handler or `onError` runs for it while it still gets one access-log line.

#### Rationale

Covers D-166 for the in-memory adapter and the wave decisions on keep-alive at shutdown and on the lifecycle gate. The TinyHttpd keep-alive socket case and T44 are wave 5 (`carried-into-wave-5.md`). D-134 maps a request arriving during `STOPPING` to `503`.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A request sent through the in-memory adapter from a before-wait shutdown participant (after `STOPPING` began, before the adapter stop) is answered `503` with `Connection: close` and `{"error":{"code":"SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE",...}}`. [D-166; D-134; wave decision: Keep-alive at shutdown]
- [x] AC-2: For that request a recording global middleware, the route handler and a recording `onError` record no call, and the access log records exactly one line with status `503`. [wave decision: Lifecycle gate; D-173]
- [x] AC-3: A request that was in flight when `STOPPING` began is not answered `503` and completes with its handler's response. [D-166; D-162] Decision: the gate reads the state once, when the request enters it. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-7 SDS-AC-23]
- [x] AC-4: The lifecycle gate runs before the pre-request checks, so during `STOPPING` an `OPTIONS *`, an unrecognised method token and a raw path not starting with `/` are each answered `503` with `Connection: close` and the standard error body, and in `STARTING` they are held on the start gate like any request. [wave decision: Lifecycle gate; D-428] Decision: the lifecycle gate is the first check of the exchange handler that `listen()` passes to the adapter, which wraps the dispatcher (FR-APP-009 AC-8).

### FR-APP-014 — Logger failures never interrupt requests or the server

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, logging |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/7 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall catch every `RuntimeException` thrown by the app logger or a log writer when the framework logs (access log, developer warnings, error logging, lifecycle logging, flush and close), so request processing, `listen()` and `shutdown()` complete as they would with a working logger.

#### Rationale

Covers D-171.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: With an app logger whose six methods all throw `RuntimeException`, a request to a route is answered with its handler's response, a handler exception is still answered with the standard `500` body, and an unfinished response is still finished. [D-171] Decision: every app logger call by the framework goes through a wrapper that drops a `RuntimeException` the logger throws. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-8 SDS-AC-1]
- [x] AC-2: With that logger, `listen(0)` returns `RUNNING`, and `shutdown` reaches `TERMINATED` even when the logger's `flush` and `close` also throw. [D-171]
- [x] AC-3: No exception thrown by the logger reaches the adapter's `ExchangeHandler` caller or the thread that called `listen` or `shutdown`. [D-171]

### ~~FR-APP-015 — One access log line per HTTP request~~ [DISCARDED → see FR-APP-019]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, access-log |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/7 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall write through the app logger at the configured level exactly one access log line per HTTP request that reaches the dispatcher, when the response completes or is aborted, in the format `<method> <path> <status>[ aborted][ <duration>ms][ in=<bytesIn> out=<bytesOut>][ rid=<requestId>]`, where `path` is the raw path without the query, the optional parts follow the options of IR-APP-007, the byte fields count body bytes actually read from and written to the connection, and requests to an excluded path produce no line.

#### Rationale

Covers D-173 (HTTP part of T38), D-174, D-177, D-135 and the D-181 decision on the line format, plus the wave decisions that the three pre-request rejections and the two lifecycle-gate `503`s also get exactly one line; only TinyHttpd's transport-level rejections (D-400) have none.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: With the defaults, `GET /users/7` answered `200` with a 5-byte body produces exactly one `info` line matching `GET /users/7 200 \d+ms in=0 out=5 rid=[0-9a-f]{16}`. [D-173; D-181; T38] Decision: this is the line format, and the request id is 16 lowercase hex characters generated per request. Decision: the duration is the `System.nanoTime()` difference from request start to response end divided by 1 000 000, in whole milliseconds. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-8 SDS-AC-4]
- [x] AC-2: With `includeDuration(false)`, `includeBytes(false)` and `includeRequestId(false)` the line is `GET /users/7 200`, and with `level(LogLevel.DEBUG)` it is written through `debug`. [D-172; D-173] Decision: a disabled part is dropped together with its leading space, and `level` selects the `Logger` method, `trace` to `error(String)` for `TRACE` to `ERROR`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-8 SDS-AC-5]
- [x] AC-3: A response aborted after commit (handler throws while `STREAMING`) produces one line with its committed status followed by ` aborted`; a `404`, a `405`, a handler `500`, each pre-request rejection (`501` unknown token, `400` raw path, `400` validator, `204` `OPTIONS *`) and each lifecycle-gate `503` produce exactly one line. [D-173; wave decision: Pre-request rejections; wave decision: Lifecycle gate]
- [x] AC-4: For a streaming response the line is written once after the stream copy finished, with `out` equal to the bytes copied; for a `HEAD` response `out` is `0`. [D-173; D-177]
- [x] AC-5: `in` counts the request body bytes the core read from the adapter's body stream (not the declared `Content-Length`): a dispatcher test whose handler reads 4 bytes of a 10-byte body through the internal `BodyState` stream logs `in=4`, and a handler that never reads gives `in=0`. [D-177]
- [x] AC-6: After `exclude("/health")`, `GET /health` produces no line while `GET /health/x` and `GET /healthz` do; with `enabled(false)` no request produces a line. [D-174; D-172] Decision: `exclude` matches the raw path without the query exactly.
- [x] AC-7: The `rid` value of the line equals the `requestId` of the standard error body of the same request. [D-135]

### SEC-APP-001 — Access log never records secrets and escapes control characters

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | security |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, access-log |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/7 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall keep `Authorization` and `Cookie` header values, passwords, tokens, request and response bodies, multipart content and the query string out of the access log line, and escape CR, LF and every other control character in each logged value.

#### Rationale

Covers D-175 and D-176 (T39).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A request `POST /login?token=qzv&password=pwz` with `Authorization: Bearer s3cr3t`, `Cookie: sid=c00k1e` and body `password=pwq2` whose handler answers `token-xyz` produces a line that contains none of `qzv`, `pwz`, `s3cr3t`, `c00k1e`, `pwq2`, `token-xyz` or `?`. [D-175; T39] Decision: the line is built only from the method token, the raw path without the query, the status, the duration and byte counts and the request id. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-8 SDS-AC-11]
- [x] AC-2: A raw path containing `%0d%0a` is logged with the raw percent-encoded form, and an unrecognised method token or path containing a CR, LF, TAB, `0x01`, `0x7F` or `0x85` character is logged with `\r`, `\n`, `\t` and `\u0001`, `\u007f`, `\u0085`, and `\` as `\\`, so every access log record is one line. [D-176; T39] Decision: control characters U+0000–U+001F, U+007F and U+0080–U+009F are escaped this way. Decision: other escaped characters are written as `\u` plus four lowercase hex digits, and percent-encoded text stays raw. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-8 SDS-AC-12]
- [x] AC-3: The access log line holds no header value at all. [D-175]
