### FR-APP-016 — Logcat logger through reflection on android.util.Log

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, logging |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/7 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall provide in `NaruServe-logcat` the public `com.snoworca.naruserve.logcat.LogcatLogger implements Logger` that maps `trace`, `debug`, `info`, `warn`, `error(String)` and `error(String, Throwable)` to `android.util.Log.v`, `d`, `i`, `w`, `e(String, String)` and `e(String, String, Throwable)` looked up by reflection, with the default tag `NaruServe` and a constructor taking a tag.

#### Rationale

Covers D-403 and the D-181 decision on the Logcat tag. The module builds without an Android SDK (wave 1). `RestApp.create()` choosing it on Android is D-402, wave 5.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A JVM test in `NaruServe-logcat` with a test-scope fake `android.util.Log` that records calls shows `new LogcatLogger()` calling `v`, `d`, `i`, `w`, `e(tag, msg)` and `e(tag, msg, t)` with tag `NaruServe` for the six methods. [D-403] Decision: the default tag is `NaruServe`. Decision: the `android.util.Log` methods are resolved once, in the constructor, through `Class.forName("android.util.Log")`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-9 SDS-AC-1]
- [x] AC-2: `new LogcatLogger("MyApp")` uses tag `MyApp`, and `new LogcatLogger(null)` throws `NullPointerException`. [D-403] Decision: the `null` tag check runs before the class lookup. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-9 SDS-AC-2]
- [x] AC-3: Without `android.util.Log` on the class path, the `LogcatLogger` constructor throws `IllegalStateException` naming `android.util.Log`; an exception thrown by a reflective call is caught and not rethrown. The case without `android.util.Log` loads `LogcatLogger` through a dedicated class loader that sees the `NaruServe-logcat` and `NaruServe-api` main classes but not the test classes. [D-403; D-171] Decision: the dedicated class loader is a `URLClassLoader` over the `NaruServe-logcat` and `NaruServe-api` main class directories whose parent is the bootstrap loader. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-9 SDS-AC-4]
- [x] AC-4: The `NaruServe-logcat` main classes reference no `android.*` type in their bytecode; `android.util.Log` appears only as a string for reflection, and the fake class lives only in the test source set. [D-403; D-178]

### FR-APP-017 — Rotating file logger with a bounded queue

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, logging |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/7 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall provide in `NaruServe-filelog` the public `com.snoworca.naruserve.filelog.RotatingFileLogger implements Logger, Flushable, AutoCloseable`, built with `RotatingFileLogger.builder()` (`directory`, `maxFileBytes`, `maxFiles`, `queueCapacity`, `level`, `build()`), that hands records to one writer thread through a bounded queue with a drop counter, rotates files, defaults to directory `./logs`, 5 MiB per file and 5 files, and stops its writer thread in `close()`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-180, D-179 (module placement), the wave decisions on logger shutdown (implements `AutoCloseable`, no writer thread outlives shutdown, I-015) and on the file logger directory (Android apps pass a writable directory; an unwritable one drops and counts records without stopping the HTTP service).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `RotatingFileLogger.builder().build()` uses directory `./logs`, `maxFileBytes` `5242880`, `maxFiles` `5`, `queueCapacity` `1024` and level `INFO`; `maxFileBytes`, `maxFiles` or `queueCapacity` below `1` throw `IllegalArgumentException`. [D-180; I-012] Decision: the queue capacity defaults to 1024 records and no value may be unlimited. Decision: an out-of-range value throws from the builder setter. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-9 SDS-AC-6]
- [x] AC-2: Records are written to `naruserve.log` in the console line format of IR-APP-006; when the next record would exceed `maxFileBytes` the files rotate to `naruserve.log.1` up to `naruserve.log.<maxFiles-1>`, dropping the oldest, so a test with `maxFileBytes(200)` and `maxFiles(3)` ends with at most three files. [D-180] Decision: the file names are `naruserve.log` and `naruserve.log.<n>`.
- [x] AC-3: With the writer blocked on a latch inside the test output stream and `queueCapacity(2)`, the third and later records are dropped without blocking the caller and `droppedCount()` counts them. [D-180]
- [x] AC-4: With a directory that cannot be created, logging calls return normally and `droppedCount()` grows; no exception reaches the caller. [wave decision: File logger directory; D-171] Decision: a failed write is handled the same way: the writer thread drops and counts the record. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-9 SDS-AC-9]
- [x] AC-5: `flush()` returns after every record queued before the call is written; `close()` writes the queued records, stops the writer thread so no thread of the logger remains, and later logging calls are dropped and counted. [wave decision: Logger shutdown; I-015] Decision: `flush()` and `close()` wait for a marker record queued behind the earlier records. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-9 SDS-AC-10]
- [x] AC-6: The module's main classes reference no SLF4J or `android.*` type and pass the API 21 animal-sniffer check. [D-179; C-02]
- [x] AC-7: The tests of this requirement and the failed-`listen()` writer-thread check of FR-APP-010 AC-6 live in the `NaruServe-filelog` test source set, which declares `testImplementation testFixtures(project(':NaruServe-api'))`; tests block the writer through a package-private constructor that takes the factory of the file output stream. [D-180; I-015]
- [x] AC-8: When renaming `naruserve.log` fails during rotation, the logger keeps appending to the same file without deleting any line, the public `rotationFailureCount()` counts the failure, the next rotation is attempted only after the file grows by another `maxFileBytes`, and a backup is deleted only when no free backup number is left, so a rename that keeps failing deletes no further backup. [D-180; D-171; wave-4 loop P issue w4-P1-001] Decision: a failed rotation keeps logging and backs off instead of retrying on every record.

### FR-APP-018 — SLF4J logger adapter

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | low |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, logging |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/7 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall provide in the JVM-only module `NaruServe-slf4j` the public `com.snoworca.naruserve.slf4j.Slf4jLogger implements Logger` that forwards the six methods to `org.slf4j.Logger` `trace`, `debug`, `info`, `warn`, `error(String)` and `error(String, Throwable)`, with a constructor taking an `org.slf4j.Logger` and a no-argument constructor using `LoggerFactory.getLogger("NaruServe")`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-179 (the SLF4J adapter in its optional module, T40).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A test with a recording `org.slf4j.Logger` shows each of the six methods forwarded once to the matching SLF4J method with the same message and throwable. [D-179]
- [x] AC-2: `new Slf4jLogger()` delegates to the SLF4J logger named `NaruServe`, checked through a hand-written test-scope `org.slf4j.impl.StaticLoggerBinder` whose `ILoggerFactory` records the requested names (slf4j-api 1.7.36 binding mechanism), which leaves the runtime dependencies binding-free, and `new Slf4jLogger(null)` throws `NullPointerException`. [D-179]
- [x] AC-3: `Slf4jLogger` lives only in `NaruServe-slf4j`, and `NaruServe-api` contains no class of the package `com.snoworca.naruserve.slf4j`. [D-179; T40]
- [x] AC-4: The recording `org.slf4j.Logger` is a hand-written implementation in the `NaruServe-slf4j` test source set, so the module adds no SLF4J binding to its runtime dependencies. [D-179]

### CON-APP-001 — NaruServe-api stays free of SLF4J and android.* while logger implementations live in their modules

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | constraint |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, logging |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/7 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall keep `NaruServe-api` free of any dependency on or bytecode reference to SLF4J (`org.slf4j`) or `android.*`, with the SLF4J adapter in `NaruServe-slf4j`, the rotating file logger in `NaruServe-filelog` and the Logcat logger in `NaruServe-logcat`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-178 and D-179 (T40), in line with the wave 1 module declarations.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The `NaruServe-api` runtime and compile class paths contain no `org.slf4j` artifact, and a bytecode scan of the `NaruServe-api` main classes finds no reference to an `org.slf4j` or `android` type. The root build's class-reference check for `:NaruServe-api` gains the prefix `org/slf4j/`. [D-178; T40]
- [x] AC-2: `RotatingFileLogger`, `Slf4jLogger` and `LogcatLogger` are absent from the `NaruServe-api` jar and present in the jars of `NaruServe-filelog`, `NaruServe-slf4j` and `NaruServe-logcat`. [D-179; T40] Decision: the root build task `checkLoggerPlacement` fails when a logger class is in the `NaruServe-api` jar or missing from its module jar. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-9 SDS-AC-15]
- [x] AC-3: The checks run in `./gradlew build`. [D-178]

### ~~OPS-APP-001 — Wave 4 decisions recorded in docs/api and their markers removed~~ [DISCARDED → see OPS-APP-003]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | operational |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, docs |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/5 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall record every wave 4 decision in `docs/api` (app, middleware, lifecycle, logging, routing, express-migration and README pages), remove the (미확정) markers that #5, #6 and #7 own, and keep the remaining markers of later waves, with each decision and its reason stated in the wave 4 commit.

#### Rationale

Covers D-137, D-151, D-167, D-181, the `docs/api/lifecycle.md` part of D-438, the wave decisions on overload `503`, `STARTING`, JVM lifetime, logger shutdown, the file logger directory and `RestApp.create()` (the `AppFactory` SPI documented beside `RestApp.builder()`), and I-008 and I-013.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `docs/api/app.md` and `docs/api/README.md` document `RestApp.builder()` with `httpAdapter`, `jsonCodec`, `logger` and `build()`, the `AppFactory` SPI and the missing-module exception of `RestApp.create()`, the route-middleware overload return type, `LifecycleCallback` and `ErrorHandler` with `throws Exception`, `ServerHandle#port()`, `address()` and `state()`, and that server threads keep the JVM alive after `main` returns until `shutdown` (a documentation statement in wave 4; the TinyHttpd adapter enforces it in wave 5), and no longer mark these as (미확정). [D-137; D-167; wave decision: RestApp.create(); wave decision: JVM lifetime]
- [x] AC-2: `docs/api/middleware.md` and `docs/api/routing.md` document `RouteBuilder.use(middleware)` as the way to attach several route middleware, the warning-and-`end()` finishing of D-126, `ErrorHandler`, `HttpException#status()`/`code()` and `UnsupportedMediaTypeException`, the `onError` chain rule, the `code` table of FR-APP-006, that `404`/`405` bodies are not configurable in v1, the row `STOPPING` → `503` with the standard body, and the full-queue `503` as a TinyHttpd transport-level plain-text answer, and drop the matching markers. [D-137; D-134; wave decision: Overload 503]
- [x] AC-3: `docs/api/lifecycle.md` documents `registerExternal`, `AppStartException`, the start gate (a request arriving in `STARTING` waits until `RUNNING` and gets `503` with `Connection: close` if the start fails) and that a `shutdown` during `STARTING` whose timeout passes returns normally while the pending stop stays in force, and drops the matching markers. [D-151; D-438; D-159; wave decision: STARTING]
- [x] AC-4: `docs/api/logging.md` documents the access log line format, the option defaults, the byte fields as body bytes actually read and written, the Logcat tag `NaruServe`, the console logger format, the file logger defaults and file names, that Android apps pass a writable directory such as `context.getFilesDir()`, that shutdown flushes and closes the app logger, and that a logger shared by several apps needs a delegate whose `close()` does nothing, and drops its marker. [D-181; D-177; wave decision: Logger shutdown; wave decision: File logger directory] Decision: the documented line form is `<method> <path> <status>[ aborted][ <n>ms][ in=<i> out=<o>][ rid=<id>]`, and the file logger defaults are `./logs`, 5 MiB per file and 5 files. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-10 SDS-AC-7]
- [x] AC-5: `docs/api/express-migration.md` documents route middleware through `RouteBuilder.use`, that `req.path()` keeps the full path under `app.use("/api", mw)`, the `500` `code` `INTERNAL_ERROR` and the absence of custom `404` bodies, and drops those markers. [D-137]
- [x] AC-6: A docs test asserts that none of the markers listed above remains and that the markers owned by later waves (for example `ws`, `staticFiles`, `multipart`, cookies, SSE, the LAN binding API) are still present. The `express.Router()` and `cors()` markers of `express-migration.md` stay (not decided in wave 4), and the lines of `app.md` that mix a wave 4 marker with a `ws` marker are split so that the `ws` return-type and `ws` callback-name markers stay while the route-middleware and `onStart`/`onStop`/`onError` parts are decided. [D-137; I-008] Decision: `AppDocsTest` keeps a removed-phrase list that fails when a listed phrase still carries `(미확정)` on its line, and a kept-phrase list that fails when a listed phrase lost its marker. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-10 SDS-AC-5]
- [x] AC-7: Next to each wave 4 decision of AC-1 to AC-5, `docs/api` states its reason in one sentence (for example why `RouteBuilder.use` instead of a varargs overload), so the wave 4 commit can quote each decision with its reason. [I-013; D-137]

### OPS-APP-002 — docs/api Java examples compile against the wave 4 API

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | operational |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-4, feasibility:high, docs |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/5 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall compile every `docs/api` Java block that uses only wave 1 to 4 API in the root docs-examples compile test, removing from its skip list each entry whose block now compiles, while blocks that need a later-wave type or method stay skipped with a named reason.

#### Rationale

Covers I-008 for the wave 4 public API, building on the wave 3 docs compile gate.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The skip entries whose reason is a wave 4 method or type (`RestApp.builder()`, `RestApp.create()`, `listen`, `shutdown`, `use`, `onStart`, `onStop`, `onError`, `Middleware`, `Logger`, `accessLog`, `ApplicationContext`) are removed, or re-pointed to the later-wave member the block still needs; entries whose only reason is `RestApp.create()` are removed because the static method compiles in wave 4; the docs-examples compile test passes in `./gradlew build`. [I-008]
- [x] AC-2: A block that still needs a later-wave member (for example `res.json`, `req.body`, `app.ws`, `staticFiles` or `Cookie`) stays on the skip list with the name of that member, and the test fails for a skip entry whose block compiles. [I-008]

### CON-APP-002 — Every new RestApp or RouteBuilder configuration method calls the NEW-state guard first

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | constraint |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall make every `RestApp` and `RouteBuilder` configuration method that a later wave adds (such as `ws`, `staticFiles`, `multipart` and `jsonCodec`) call the internal guard `AppLifecycle.requireConfigurable` before it records anything.

#### Rationale

Durable rule of SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-3 (app isolation, NEW-state guard, listen() startup and start gate), moved to the SRS at SDS close; binds FR-APP-002.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The reflection test of FR-APP-002 AC-4 lists every non-static `RestApp` method returning `RestApp` and every `RouteBuilder` registration method, including those a later wave adds, and finds each one rejected with `IllegalStateException` in `RUNNING` with nothing recorded.

### CON-APP-003 — Exceptions leave next.run() unchanged and reach the error path once

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | constraint |
| Target | wave-4 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall let an exception thrown downstream leave `next.run()` unchanged and shall hand it to the error path exactly once, from the dispatcher, so a middleware can wrap `next.run()` in `try`/`catch` like a Servlet filter.

#### Rationale

Durable rule of SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-4 (middleware pipeline, next contract, dispatcher order), moved to the SRS at SDS close; binds FR-APP-004 and FR-APP-005.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A middleware that wraps `next.run()` in `try`/`catch` catches the same exception instance the handler threw, and when it rethrows that instance a recording `onError` receives it exactly once.

#### Verification Evidence

### CON-APP-005 — The error outcome follows the response state after the onError chain and committed responses are aborted

| Field | Value |

| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall decide the outcome of the error path by the response state after the `onError` chain, and shall abort a committed response instead of giving it a second status line.

#### Rationale

Durable rule of SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-5 (error path and standard error body), moved to the SRS at SDS close; binds FR-APP-005.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A handler that throws while the response is `STREAMING` leaves the response `ABORTED` with no second status line written, and a handler that throws before commit, with no `onError` handler responding, gets the standard error response of FR-APP-006.

#### Requirement

The system shall keep every wave 4 decision recorded in `docs/api` (app, middleware, lifecycle, logging, routing, express-migration and README pages) without the (미확정) markers that #5, #6 and #7 own, describe the JVM lifetime through the TinyHttpd adapter's non-daemon keeper thread, and keep the remaining markers of later waves except those that the wave 5 requirements OPS-ENGINE-001, OPS-JSON-001 and OPS-COOKIE-001 remove.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `docs/api/app.md` and `docs/api/README.md` document `RestApp.builder()` with `httpAdapter`, `jsonCodec`, `logger` and `build()`, the `AppFactory` SPI and the missing-module exception of `RestApp.create()`, the route-middleware overload return type, `LifecycleCallback` and `ErrorHandler` with `throws Exception`, `ServerHandle#port()`, `address()` and `state()`, and that one non-daemon keeper thread of the TinyHttpd adapter keeps the JVM alive after `main` returns until `shutdown` while TinyHttpd's own threads stay daemon, and no longer mark these as (미확정). [D-137; D-167; wave decision: RestApp.create(); wave decision: JVM lifetime; wave-5 wave decision: Thread wording] re_stated_from: OPS-APP-001#AC1 (JVM-lifetime wording changed by wave 5)
- [x] AC-2: `docs/api/middleware.md` and `docs/api/routing.md` document `RouteBuilder.use(middleware)` as the way to attach several route middleware, the warning-and-`end()` finishing of D-126, `ErrorHandler`, `HttpException#status()`/`code()` and `UnsupportedMediaTypeException`, the `onError` chain rule, the `code` table of FR-APP-006, that `404`/`405` bodies are not configurable in v1, the row `STOPPING` → `503` with the standard body, and the full-queue `503` as a TinyHttpd transport-level plain-text answer, and drop the matching markers. [D-137; D-134; wave decision: Overload 503] re_stated_from: OPS-APP-001#AC2
- [x] AC-3: `docs/api/lifecycle.md` documents `registerExternal`, `AppStartException`, the start gate (a request arriving in `STARTING` waits until `RUNNING` and gets `503` with `Connection: close` if the start fails) and that a `shutdown` during `STARTING` whose timeout passes returns normally while the pending stop stays in force, and drops the matching markers. [D-151; D-438; D-159; wave decision: STARTING] re_stated_from: OPS-APP-001#AC3
- [x] AC-4: `docs/api/logging.md` documents the access log line format, the option defaults, the byte fields as body bytes actually read and written, the Logcat tag `NaruServe`, the console logger format, the file logger defaults and file names, that Android apps pass a writable directory such as `context.getFilesDir()`, that shutdown flushes and closes the app logger, and that a logger shared by several apps needs a delegate whose `close()` does nothing, and drops its marker; the documented line form is `<method> <path> <status>[ aborted][ <n>ms][ in=<i> out=<o>][ rid=<id>]`, and the file logger defaults are `./logs`, 5 MiB per file and 5 files. [D-181; D-177; wave decision: Logger shutdown; wave decision: File logger directory] re_stated_from: OPS-APP-001#AC4
- [x] AC-5: `docs/api/express-migration.md` documents route middleware through `RouteBuilder.use`, that `req.path()` keeps the full path under `app.use("/api", mw)`, the `500` `code` `INTERNAL_ERROR` and the absence of custom `404` bodies, and drops those markers. [D-137] re_stated_from: OPS-APP-001#AC5
- [x] AC-6: `AppDocsTest`, re-pointed to this requirement, keeps a removed-phrase list that fails when a listed phrase still carries `(미확정)` on its line and a kept-phrase list that fails when a listed phrase lost its marker; the kept list holds the markers of waves 6 to 8 (for example `ws`, `staticFiles`, `multipart`, the SSE send queue, the WebSocket idle, ping and handshake limits and the `ws` return-type and `ws` callback-name markers of `app.md`) and the `express.Router()` and `cors()` markers of `express-migration.md`, while every entry wave 5 decides moves to the removed list: the cookie markers, the LAN binding API line of `app.md`, the `express-migration.md` rows for trust proxy and for the 0.0.0.0 binding, and the `docs/api/defaults.md` rows for the bind address (바인딩 주소) and the proxy headers (프록시 헤더). [D-137; I-008; wave-5: OPS-ENGINE-001; wave-5: OPS-COOKIE-001] re_stated_from: OPS-APP-001#AC6 (kept-marker set changed by wave 5)
- [x] AC-7: Next to each wave 4 decision of AC-1 to AC-5, `docs/api` states its reason in one sentence (for example why `RouteBuilder.use` instead of a varargs overload), so the wave 4 commit can quote each decision with its reason. [I-013; D-137] re_stated_from: OPS-APP-001#AC7

#### Requirement

The system shall keep every wave 4 decision recorded in `docs/api` (app, middleware, lifecycle, logging, routing, express-migration and README pages) without the (미확정) markers that #5, #6 and #7 own, describe the JVM lifetime through the TinyHttpd adapter's non-daemon keeper thread, remove the static-file and multipart markers that wave 6 decides, and keep the remaining markers of waves 7 and 8.

### FR-APP-019 — Access log: one line per HTTP request, the WebSocket handshake and close lines, and the SSE stream-end line (wave 7)

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | wave-7 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-7, feasibility:high, access-log, supersedes:FR-APP-015 |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/15 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall write through the app logger at the configured level exactly one access log line per HTTP request that reaches the dispatcher, when the response completes or is aborted, in the format `<method> <path> <status>[ aborted][ <duration>ms][ in=<bytesIn> out=<bytesOut>][ rid=<requestId>]`, where `path` is the raw path without the query, the optional parts follow the options of IR-APP-007, the byte fields count body bytes actually read from and written to the connection, and requests to an excluded path produce no line, except that an accepted WebSocket upgrade gets its line with status `101` when the connection opens plus a separate close line with close code and duration, and an SSE stream gets its one line when the stream ends, with its start time and closing cause.

#### Rationale

Restates FR-APP-015 AC-1 to AC-7 unchanged for plain HTTP (D-173, D-174, D-177, D-135, D-181 and the pre-request and lifecycle-gate decisions of wave 4) and adds D-430 (the `101` handshake line written in `onOpen`), D-325 (separate WebSocket close line with close code and duration) and D-338 (one SSE line at stream end with start time, duration, bytes and closing cause), which change the "one line per request when the response completes" statement; test T38 (WebSocket and SSE parts). Measured: `internal/AccessLog.java:51-86` formats the D-173 line once per request when the response completes; `DefaultAccessLogOptions`, `BodyState` and `RequestIds` keep their roles. Decision: the WebSocket close line starts with the token `WS` instead of an HTTP method so log tools can tell it from request lines; the SSE line keeps the D-173 prefix and appends ` sse start=<epochMillis> cause=<client|app|overflow|shutdown|error>`. Decision (SSE cause, AC-10): the first end reason recorded wins. The core records `app` when `close()` is called, `overflow` when the overflow policy ends the stream and `shutdown` when the closing participant marks the stream ended or the deadline force-close begins, before any `IOException` that the ending causes (TinyHttpd's `stop` aborts every remaining socket at the grace deadline, so the stalled drain write of FR-SSE-005 AC-5 then fails with an `IOException`); `client` is recorded only when an `IOException` or a detected closed connection ends a stream with no earlier recorded reason, since a client disconnect is seen only as such a failure (FR-SSE-003 AC-7, FR-SSE-004 AC-2); `error` is kept for a failure that is not an `IOException` (a `RuntimeException` in the drain, serialisation or adapter stream) with no earlier recorded reason; the core tells `client` from `error` by exception type. Pitfall: writing the SSE line at `res.sse()` time would log `out=0` and no cause; it is written when the stream ends. Pitfall: an upgrade rejected before `onOpen` must not get a `101` line; it takes the ordinary error-path line (D-444). Rejected alternative: logging every WebSocket message; it would flood the log of a chat app, reopen on demand as an option. Tests to re-point to FR-APP-019: every `FR_APP_015_*` method of `NaruServe-api/src/test/java/com/snoworca/naruserve/internal/AccessLogTest.java`, `AccessLogAcceptanceTest.java`, `internal/DispatcherTest.java` (pre-request rejection log asserts) and the access-log asserts of `internal/StoppingGateTest.java` and `internal/AppIsolationTest.java`; the `@req FR-APP-015` comments of `AccessLog.java`, `BodyState.java`, `DefaultAccessLogOptions.java`, `DefaultResponse.java`, `DefaultRestApp.java`, `Dispatcher.java` and `RequestIds.java` move to FR-APP-019.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: With the defaults, `GET /users/7` answered `200` with a 5-byte body produces exactly one `info` line matching `GET /users/7 200 \d+ms in=0 out=5 rid=[0-9a-f]{16}`. [D-173; D-181; T38] Decision: this is the line format, and the request id is 16 lowercase hex characters generated per request. Decision: the duration is the `System.nanoTime()` difference from request start to response end divided by 1 000 000, in whole milliseconds. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-8 SDS-AC-4] re_stated_from: FR-APP-015#AC1
- [x] AC-2: With `includeDuration(false)`, `includeBytes(false)` and `includeRequestId(false)` the line is `GET /users/7 200`, and with `level(LogLevel.DEBUG)` it is written through `debug`. [D-172; D-173] Decision: a disabled part is dropped together with its leading space, and `level` selects the `Logger` method, `trace` to `error(String)` for `TRACE` to `ERROR`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-4-8 SDS-AC-5] re_stated_from: FR-APP-015#AC2
- [x] AC-3: A response aborted after commit (handler throws while `STREAMING`) produces one line with its committed status followed by ` aborted`; a `404`, a `405`, a handler `500`, each pre-request rejection (`501` unknown token, `400` raw path, `400` validator, `204` `OPTIONS *`) and each lifecycle-gate `503` produce exactly one line. [D-173; wave decision: Pre-request rejections; wave decision: Lifecycle gate] re_stated_from: FR-APP-015#AC3
- [x] AC-4: For a streaming response the line is written once after the stream copy finished, with `out` equal to the bytes copied; for a `HEAD` response `out` is `0`. [D-173; D-177] re_stated_from: FR-APP-015#AC4
- [x] AC-5: `in` counts the request body bytes the core read from the adapter's body stream (not the declared `Content-Length`): a dispatcher test whose handler reads 4 bytes of a 10-byte body through the internal `BodyState` stream logs `in=4`, and a handler that never reads gives `in=0`. [D-177] re_stated_from: FR-APP-015#AC5
- [x] AC-6: After `exclude("/health")`, `GET /health` produces no line while `GET /health/x` and `GET /healthz` do; with `enabled(false)` no request produces a line. [D-174; D-172] Decision: `exclude` matches the raw path without the query exactly. re_stated_from: FR-APP-015#AC6
- [x] AC-7: The `rid` value of the line equals the `requestId` of the standard error body of the same request. [D-135] re_stated_from: FR-APP-015#AC7
- [x] AC-8: An accepted upgrade to `/ws` produces exactly one handshake line matching `GET /ws 101 \d+ms in=0 out=0 rid=[0-9a-f]{16}`, written in the endpoint's `onOpen` after `101` was written and before the first frame is read, which a test asserts by reading the log before the client sends its first frame; an upgrade rejected before `onOpen` (`426` for `Sec-WebSocket-Version: 12`, `403` for a refused `Origin`, `503` beyond the limit) produces its ordinary line with the rejection status and no `101` line (T38). [D-430; D-325; T38] Decision: the handshake line is written in `onOpen` after the channel is stored and before the handler, with the duration measured from `req.startNanos()`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-7-12 SDS-AC-1] Decision: when the response ends with `upgraded101()` true no line is written from `onDone`, and a refusal before `onOpen` never calls `upgraded`, so its ordinary line is written. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-7-12 SDS-AC-2]
- [x] AC-9: When a session ends, one close line `WS /ws <closeCode> \d+ms rid=<id>` is written with the same `rid` as its handshake line, the close code chosen by FR-WS-006 AC-9 (`1000` for `ws.close()`, `1001` at shutdown, `1008` on queue overflow, each also when the client never answers the server's close frame; the client's code for a client close; `1006` for a session aborted without any close frame) and the duration from `onOpen` to the close, exactly once per session (T38). Decision: the close line form is `WS <path> <closeCode>[ <n>ms][ rid=<id>]`. [D-325; T38]
- [ ] AC-10: An SSE stream produces no line when it starts and exactly one line when it ends, matching `GET /events 200 \d+ms in=0 out=<n> rid=[0-9a-f]{16} sse start=\d+ cause=(client|app|overflow|shutdown|error)`, where the duration covers the whole stream, `out` equals the bytes written to the stream, `start` is the epoch milliseconds of the stream start, and `cause` is the first end reason the core recorded for the stream: `app` when `close()` is called, `overflow` when the overflow policy ends the stream, and `shutdown` when the closing participant marks the stream ended or the deadline force-close begins, each recorded before any `IOException` that the ending causes; `client` only when a drain write or heartbeat to the socket failed with an `IOException`, or the drain found the connection closed, on a stream with no earlier recorded reason (how a client disconnect is detected); and `error` when a failure that is not an `IOException` in the core or app code during the drain (for example a `RuntimeException` thrown by event serialisation or by the adapter's output stream) ends a stream with no earlier recorded reason; an in-memory test asserts `cause=client` for a drain write failed with `IOException`, `cause=error` for a drain write failed with a `RuntimeException`, `cause=app` when the drain write after `close()` fails with `IOException` and `cause=overflow` when the drain write after the overflow end fails with `IOException`, and a TinyHttpd test asserts `cause=shutdown` for the stalled stream force-closed at the shutdown deadline (FR-SSE-005 AC-5); a `HEAD` to an SSE route produces its ordinary line without the `sse` part (T38). Decision: the SSE line appends ` sse start=<epochMillis> cause=<cause>` to the D-173 line, and the first recorded end reason wins over a later `IOException` or failure. [D-338; D-343; T38] Decision: for a response with a non-null `sseClient()`, `writeSse` is called with status `200`, `out` = `bytesWritten()`, the stream's start millis and the client's `cause()`; a null cause cannot occur because `sseEnded` runs only after `endBy`. [SDS 2026-10-19.naruserve.naruserve-v1-wave-7-12 SDS-AC-4] A Servlet stream aborted before drain startup still emits its terminal stream-end record once.
- [x] AC-11: `exclude("/ws")` and `exclude("/events")` suppress the WebSocket handshake and close lines and the SSE line of those paths, `enabled(false)` suppresses all of them, and `level(LogLevel.DEBUG)` writes them through `debug`. [D-325; D-338; D-174]

### FR-APP-020 — Long-connection shutdown coordination: closed flags before snapshots, participants with the deadline, the accepting phase, in-flight requests, force-close and the 3 s bound

| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall coordinate the WebSocket and SSE closing participants in the before-wait phase of shutdown by setting each registry's closed flag before its snapshot, passing each participant the shutdown deadline, running them while the adapter still accepts connections for at most 1 s with every request in that phase answered `503` with `Connection: close`, letting in-flight short requests complete before `onStop`, force-closing long connections still open at the deadline, returning from `shutdown(timeout, unit)` within the timeout plus a 3 s margin, and leaving no thread, queue or temporary upload file of the app behind.

#### Rationale

Covers D-342, D-343, D-344, D-345, D-439, D-445 (shared ordering and the `res.sse()`/upgrade answers after the flag), D-446, carried loop-D fix V2-002 (D-445, D-446), wave decision Shutdown bound (W2-010) and residual Deadline for shutdown participants (r5 W2-003); test T44. The per-registry participants are FR-WS-010 and FR-SSE-005; this requirement holds what they share. Measured: `internal/ShutdownParticipants.java:33` has only `add(Phase, Runnable)` with no deadline; `internal/DefaultRestApp.java:544` computes `deadline = System.nanoTime() + unit.toNanos(timeout)` and `:610-636` runs before-wait participants, `adapter.stop(left(deadline))`, force-close participants, `onStop`, after-on-stop participants (FR-APP-011); the stopping gate answers `503` + `Connection: close` (FR-APP-013, D-166). Decision: `ShutdownParticipants` gains an add-only overload taking a package-private functional interface `void run(long deadlineNanos)`; existing `Runnable` participants are unchanged (the type is package-private, so the change is allowed). Decision: the D-344 margin is 3 s = TinyHttpd `stop(grace)` overrun up to 1.5 s (acceptor join up to 1000 ms plus worker wait up to 500 ms) + adapter release wait of D-107 up to 1 s + 0.5 s scheduling slack, recorded in `docs/api/lifecycle.md` and used as the T44 tolerance; W2-010: the bound covers the core and adapter waits of D-162 while `onStop`, resource close and logger flush return promptly, and `docs/api/lifecycle.md` keeps its statement that those steps run without a time limit. Deviation (recorded): object.md §11.4 step 2 stops accepting new connections and upgrades before the WebSocket and SSE goodbye of step 3; NaruServe runs the before-wait participants while TinyHttpd still accepts connections (at most 1 s) and answers every request reaching the dispatcher with `503` + `Connection: close` (D-446), which `docs/api/lifecycle.md` records. Pitfall: snapshotting before setting the flag lets a session or stream register between the two and miss the goodbye (D-445). Rejected alternative: stopping the TinyHttpd acceptor before the participants; TinyHttpd's stop also closes its upgraded connections at once without the 1001 close frame, reopen if TinyHttpd gains a separate stop-accepting call (D-020).

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `ShutdownParticipants` keeps `add(Phase, Runnable)` and gains an overload taking a participant that receives `long deadlineNanos`; participants of both kinds registered in one phase run in registration order, and a deadline participant receives the deadline that `shutdown(timeout, unit)` computed, which a test asserts by checking that `deadlineNanos - System.nanoTime()` at entry is at most the timeout and at least the timeout minus 100 ms. Decision: the deadline participant is a package-private functional interface. [residual: Deadline for shutdown participants (r5 W2-003)]
- [x] AC-2: With a shutdown timeout of 500 ms and one WebSocket session whose client stopped reading, the WebSocket close wait ends within 500 ms plus 200 ms and `shutdown` returns within 500 ms plus the 3 s margin (T44 case with a timeout below 1 s). [residual: Deadline for shutdown participants (r5 W2-003)] [D-344]
- [x] AC-3: Each closing participant sets its registry's closed flag under the registry lock before it takes its snapshot; afterwards `res.sse()` in a handler that was already running returns a client that is ended at once: the client receives the event-stream headers, the closing notice `event: shutdown\ndata: shutdown\n\n` and the end of the body, the `onClose` listeners run once and `send` returns `false`; an upgrade that reaches the upgrade extension point after the flag receives `503` with `Connection: close` before any `101`; and every session or stream registered before the flag is in the snapshot, which an in-memory test asserts by setting the flag between two registrations. [D-445; carried: V2-002]
- [x] AC-4: The before-wait participants run while the adapter still accepts connections, for at most 1 s plus 200 ms of slack: a plain request sent while the WebSocket close wait is held by a stalled session receives `503` with `Connection: close` and the standard error body from the stopping gate, and an upgrade sent in that phase is refused without `101`. Deviation (recorded): object.md §11.4 step 2 stops accepting new connections and upgrades before the goodbye of step 3; NaruServe accepts connections during this phase and answers them `503` under D-166, which `docs/api/lifecycle.md` records. [D-446; carried: V2-002]
- [x] AC-5: A short request whose handler takes 300 ms and starts before `shutdown(5, TimeUnit.SECONDS)` completes with `200` and its full body, and the `onStop` callback runs only after that response completed, which a test asserts by recording both events in order (T44). [D-342]
- [x] AC-6: A WebSocket session and an SSE stream whose clients stopped reading are force-closed by the deadline: both client sockets reach end of stream or reset within the shutdown timeout plus the 3 s margin. [D-343]
- [x] AC-7: `shutdown(timeout, unit)` returns within the timeout plus 3 s when `onStop` callbacks and owned resources return at once, and `docs/api/lifecycle.md` records the 3 s margin with its parts (TinyHttpd stop overrun up to 1.5 s, adapter release up to 1 s, 0.5 s slack) and keeps the statement that `onStop`, resource close and logger flush run without a time limit. Decision: the D-344 margin is 3 s and is the T44 tolerance. [D-344; wave decision: Shutdown bound (W2-010)]
- [x] AC-8: A T44 test opens two WebSocket sessions whose clients stop reading while the server sends to them until their writes block, one healthy WebSocket session and one SSE stream over TinyHttpd, calls `shutdown(1, TimeUnit.SECONDS)` and asserts that it returns within 1 s plus 3 s, that the healthy session receives close `1001`, that the SSE client receives the closing notice and that both stalled sessions' client sockets are closed. [D-439; T44]
- [x] AC-9: After `shutdown` returns, no live thread of that app remains, counted by name as the threads whose names start with `naruserve-` plus `tinyhttpd-acceptor-<port>` and `tinyhttpd-watchdog`, the WebSocket session registry and the SSE stream registry are empty, and the multipart temporary directory holds no file created by that app, which a test run in an isolated test JVM fork that starts no other app asserts after a run with open sessions, streams and an upload in progress (T44). Decision: the app's threads are counted by name in an isolated fork, as in PERF-APP-001 AC-1, because TinyHttpd's `tinyhttpd-watchdog` name carries no port. [D-345]

### PERF-APP-001 — Long-connection load bounds: 100+ WebSocket and SSE connections within the thread total and a heap bound, documented thread and stack figures, and the low-end Android follow-up

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | performance |
| Target | wave-7 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | wave-7, feasibility:high, load |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/15 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall hold at least 100 concurrent WebSocket and SSE connections from the loopback address under the default limits with the app's thread count at most 452 and its heap growth below the bound written in the test, document the thread total and the stack figure of the default limits for API 21 devices, and record device-specific tuning of long-connection limits for low-end Android as a manual follow-up of the release test in a section of `docs/api/defaults.md`.

#### Rationale

Covers D-346 (T26), D-416, D-347, wave decision Thread total (W2-006) and its round-2 reading (r2 W1-001). Frozen D-416 bounds the threads of one app to 451: 192 TinyHttpd request workers (64 HTTP plus 128 SSE), 128 WebSocket read threads kept by upgraded connections, 128 WebSocket sender threads, one NaruServe idle-timeout scheduler thread, and TinyHttpd's acceptor and watchdog threads. Deviation (recorded): the bounded total is 452, because the wave 5 non-daemon `naruserve-keeper` thread (FR-ENGINE-006; `tinyhttpd/KeeperThread.java:11-35`) runs from bind to stop; the T26 bound and `docs/api/defaults.md` use 452 (W2-006, r2 W1-001). The stack figure is unchanged: 449 request-path threads (192 + 128 + 128 + 1) request 256 KiB each = 112.25 MiB, while the acceptor, watchdog and keeper use the platform default stack and sit outside that budget. Measured: `ServerOptions.java:41-54` defaults `maxWorkers` 64, `maxSseStreams` 128, `maxUpgradedConnections` 128 (defaults.md rows), and `TinyHttpdAdapter.java:105-116` sizes the worker pool `maxWorkers + maxSseStreams`. Decision: the T26 heap bound is 64 MiB of used-heap growth after `System.gc()` with 120 open connections, which is far below 120 full 1 MiB send queues because queues allocate only what is queued and T26 queues one message per connection. Pitfall: counting every JVM thread would include the test's own client threads; the test counts the app's threads by name prefix and by the TinyHttpd thread group. D-347 cannot be automated on a JVM; its AC is a recorded manual follow-up. Rejected alternative: a fixed WebSocket sender pool of 16 threads; D-320 forbids it (20 stalled sessions).
- [2026-10-20] Correction: the 64 MiB heap bound is far below 120 full 1 MiB send queues because queues allocate only what is queued, and T26 queues one message per connection. D-347 is recorded in the `저사양 Android 튜닝(수동 후속 작업)` section of `docs/api/defaults.md` (AC-4), a path the wave 7 docs create, so a docs test can find it.
- [2026-10-20] Correction (wave-7 feasibility): the "TinyHttpd thread group" counting above is replaced by name-based counting (AC-1): threads named `naruserve-*` (workers `naruserve-<port>-worker-<n>`, keeper, `naruserve-<port>-ws-sender-<n>`, `naruserve-<port>-ws-idle`) plus `tinyhttpd-acceptor-<port>` and `tinyhttpd-watchdog`, in an isolated test JVM fork, since the watchdog name has no port. AC-3 reads the requested stack size through an internal thread-factory hook.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: A JVM test (T26) opens 60 WebSocket sessions and 60 SSE streams from `127.0.0.1` under the default limits, each receives one message or event within 5 s, and while all 120 are open the count of live threads belonging to the app is at most 452 and the used heap after `System.gc()` grew by less than 64 MiB from the value before the connections opened. Decision: the heap bound is 64 MiB. Decision: the app's threads are counted by name, as the threads whose names start with `naruserve-` plus `tinyhttpd-acceptor-<port>` and `tinyhttpd-watchdog`, and the test runs in an isolated test JVM fork that starts no other app, because TinyHttpd's `tinyhttpd-watchdog` name carries no port. [D-346; T26]
- [x] AC-2: A test computes the thread total from the defaults as `maxWorkers + maxSseStreams` (192) + `maxUpgradedConnections` read threads (128) + `maxUpgradedConnections` sender threads (128) + 1 idle scheduler + TinyHttpd acceptor + watchdog + `naruserve-keeper` and asserts 452, and asserts that the T26 thread bound constant equals it. Frozen: D-416 bounds the threads of one app to 451. Deviation (recorded): the total is 452 because the non-daemon `naruserve-keeper` thread runs from bind to stop. [D-416; wave decision: Thread total (W2-006); wave decision: Thread total (r2 W1-001)]
- [x] AC-3: The WebSocket sender threads and the idle scheduler thread are created with a requested stack size of 262144 bytes, which a test reads through an internal hook of each thread factory that reports the stack size the factory passed to the `Thread` constructor, because a live thread does not expose its requested stack size, and `docs/api/defaults.md` states for API 21 devices the thread total 452 and that 449 request-path threads request 112.25 MiB of stack, which a docs test reads. [D-416]
- [x] AC-4: `docs/api/defaults.md` has a section headed `저사양 Android 튜닝(수동 후속 작업)` stating that device-specific tuning of `maxSseStreams`, `maxUpgradedConnections` and the WebSocket and SSE `sendQueueSize` on a low-end Android 5.0 device is a manual follow-up of the release test, not an automated result, and a docs test finds that heading and the names `maxSseStreams`, `maxUpgradedConnections` and `sendQueueSize` in that section. Manual follow-up recorded. [D-347]

### OPS-APP-005 — docs/api keeps the wave 4 decisions, removes the wave 7 markers and describes the long-connection shutdown steps

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | operational |
| Target | wave-7 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | wave-7, feasibility:high, docs, supersedes:OPS-APP-004 |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | https://github.com/Snoworca/NaruServe/issues/13 |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system shall keep every wave 4 decision recorded in `docs/api` (app, middleware, lifecycle, logging, routing, express-migration and README pages) without the (미확정) markers that #5, #6 and #7 own, describe the JVM lifetime through the TinyHttpd adapter's non-daemon keeper thread, keep the static-file and multipart markers that wave 6 removed gone, remove the WebSocket and SSE markers that wave 7 decides, describe the wave 7 shutdown steps with the long-connection goodbye, its deviation from object.md §11.4 and the 3 s margin, and keep only the `express-migration.md` markers owned by no wave of this run.

#### Rationale

Restates OPS-APP-004 AC-1 to AC-5 and AC-7 unchanged (D-137, D-167, D-134, D-151, D-438, D-159, D-181, D-177, I-013 and the wave 4 to 6 marker decisions). AC-6 changes because wave 7 settles every marker its kept list held: the `app.md` `ws` return type (`RestApp`) and callback name (`WebSocketHandler`) of IR-WS-001, the `websocket.md` idle/ping/handshake and `101` timing markers (OPS-WS-001, W2-004: the handler runs once after `101`), and the `sse.md` heartbeat and send-queue markers (OPS-SSE-001); only the `express.Router()`, `cors()` and `res.redirect(url)` rows stay marked, because no wave of this run owns them. New AC-8 rewrites the `app.md` shutdown steps for D-446 (goodbye while connections are still accepted) and new AC-9 records in `lifecycle.md` the D-446 deviation from object.md §11.4, the D-344 margin of 3 s (FR-APP-020) with the W2-010 statement kept, and r5 W2-004; it also covers the `logging.md` WebSocket and SSE rows of FR-APP-019. Measured: `NaruServe-api/src/test/java/com/snoworca/naruserve/AppDocsTest.java:51-55` holds the KEPT map (`app.md`: "`ws`의 반환 타입", "`ws`에 넘기는 콜백"; `websocket.md`: "idle timeout", "Ping 주기", "handshake timeout", "`101` 응답 전/후"; `sse.md`: "heartbeat", "연결별 송신 큐 기본값"; `express-migration.md`: Router, cors, redirect); `:168-179` `sdsAc5_OPS_APP_004_AC6_wsMarkersSitOnTheirOwnLines`; `docs/api/app.md:205-216` lists the six shutdown steps with "SSE·WebSocket에는 종료를 알립니다" in step 2. Pitfall: the removed list requires each phrase to stay present, so the rewritten `websocket.md` timing sentence carries "`101` 응답 뒤" and the other phrases stay; `sdsAc5_OPS_APP_004_AC6_wave4PagesKeepNoMarker` keeps requiring zero markers in `lifecycle.md` and `logging.md`. Rejected alternative: deleting the KEPT list entirely; the three `express-migration.md` markers would then go unguarded. Tests to re-point to OPS-APP-005: every `OPS_APP_004_*` and `sdsAc5_OPS_APP_004_*` method of `AppDocsTest` (`decidedMarkersAreGone`, `laterWaveMarkersAreKept`, `wave4PagesKeepNoMarker`, `wsMarkersSitOnTheirOwnLines`, AC1 to AC5 and AC7), with the KEPT list cut to the three `express-migration.md` rows, the wave 7 phrases added to REMOVED, and new methods for AC-8 and AC-9.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `docs/api/app.md` and `docs/api/README.md` document `RestApp.builder()` with `httpAdapter`, `jsonCodec`, `logger` and `build()`, the `AppFactory` SPI and the missing-module exception of `RestApp.create()`, the route-middleware overload return type, `LifecycleCallback` and `ErrorHandler` with `throws Exception`, `ServerHandle#port()`, `address()` and `state()`, and that one non-daemon keeper thread of the TinyHttpd adapter keeps the JVM alive after `main` returns until `shutdown` while TinyHttpd's own threads stay daemon, and no longer mark these as (미확정). [D-137; D-167; wave decision: RestApp.create(); wave decision: JVM lifetime; wave-5 wave decision: Thread wording] re_stated_from: OPS-APP-004#AC1

[D-151; D-438; D-159; wave decision: STARTING] re_stated_from: OPS-APP-004#AC3 Current contract: all undecided markers are removed; group, CORS and redirect are implemented and documented. The kept list holds only the `| `express.Router()``, `| `cors()`` and `| `res.redirect(url)`` rows of `express-migration.md`. The removed list keeps every entry that waves 4, 5 and 6 decided and gains every entry that wave 7 decides: in `app.md` `` `ws`의 반환 타입 `` and `` `ws`에 넘기는 콜백 ``; in `websocket.md` `idle timeout`, `Ping 주기`, `handshake timeout` and `` `101` 응답 뒤 `` (the phrase that replaces `` `101` 응답 전/후 ``); in `sse.md` `heartbeat` and `연결별 송신 큐 기본값`; and `sdsAc5_OPS_APP_004_AC6_wsMarkersSitOnTheirOwnLines` becomes a check that each of the two `ws` phrases of `app.md` sits on exactly one line without a marker. [D-137; I-008; D-303; D-419; D-339; wave decision: Handler timing (W2-004)] re_stated_from: OPS-APP-004#AC6 (changed by wave 7: the wave 7 markers move from the kept list to the removed list) Current contract: all undecided markers are removed; group, CORS and redirect are implemented and documented. FR-DX-001 and FR-DX-007 override former attribute-null casts and JSON reread restrictions; FR-DX-008 overrides global-before-prefix execution.
- [x] AC-7: Next to each wave 4 decision of AC-1 to AC-5, `docs/api` states its reason in one sentence (for example why `RouteBuilder.use` instead of a varargs overload), so the wave 4 commit can quote each decision with its reason. [I-013; D-137] re_stated_from: OPS-APP-004#AC7
- [x] AC-8: The `shutdown` steps of `docs/api/app.md` state, in this order: `STOPPING` begins and every new request or WebSocket upgrade that reaches the app is answered `503` with `Connection: close`; open SSE streams receive the closing notice and open WebSocket sessions receive close `1001`, for at most 1 s while connections are still accepted; the server stops accepting and waits for in-flight requests until the timeout; connections still open at the timeout are force-closed; then `onStop`, closing owned resources and the logger flush as before; the sentence on what the timeout covers (today `timeout은 2단계(진행 중인 요청을 기다리는 시간)에만 걸리고`) names steps 2 to 4 (the goodbye, which waits at most 1 s or the time left, the wait for in-flight requests and the force-close at the timeout) as sharing the one timeout of D-344, instead of `2단계`, and keeps that `onStop`, closing owned resources and the logger flush have no time limit; a docs test finds each step by a fixed phrase in that order and finds that sentence naming `2~4단계`. [D-446; D-340; D-341; D-342; D-343]
- [x] AC-9: `docs/api/lifecycle.md` states that the WebSocket and SSE goodbye runs while connections are still accepted, for at most 1 s, and that this differs from object.md §11.4, whose step 2 stops accepting before the goodbye of step 3; that `shutdown(timeout, unit)` returns within the timeout plus 3 s (TinyHttpd stop overrun up to 1.5 s, adapter release up to 1 s, 0.5 s slack) when `onStop` and resources return at once, keeping the sentence that `onStop`, resource close and logger flush run without a time limit; and that `app.shutdown` called from an SSE `onClose` listener returns at once like a call from a request handler; and `docs/api/logging.md` documents the WebSocket handshake and close lines and the SSE stream-end line of FR-APP-019 without a marker; a docs test finds each statement by a fixed phrase. [D-446; D-344; wave decision: Shutdown bound (W2-010); residual: shutdown from an SSE onClose listener (r5 W2-004); D-325; D-338] Current contract: all undecided markers are removed; group, CORS and redirect are implemented and documented. FR-DX-001 and FR-DX-007 override former attribute-null casts and JSON reread restrictions; FR-DX-008 overrides global-before-prefix execution.
